# C01A | DKS synthesis/primer files A (10-K key facts, alt data, conferences, deal/pro forma/debt docs, earnings calls, expert calls, 10-K/10-Q financials, Bloomberg actuals & consensus)
> SOURCES: 8 files | DKS FY2025 = FYE 2026-01-31 (Bloomberg labels this "2026 Y"); FY2026 = FYE 2027-01-30; FL acquired 2025-09-08 | legend: [d]=derived by note-writer, [r]=table rebuilt from garbled PDF text, =same as=duplicate pointer; "source-derived"/DERIVED = arithmetic done by the original compiler

## SRC 00_DKS_SYNTHESIS/DKS_10K_Key_Facts_and_Risk_Factors.md | n.d. (covers FY2025 10-K filed 2026-03-27) | synthesis of FY2025/FY2024/FY2023/FY2022 10-Ks | FY2022-FY2025
- Primary src FY2025 10-K (`01_DKS_SEC_FILINGS/10-K/DKS_10-K_FY2025_filed-2026-03-27.md`); priors FY2024 10-K (filed 2025-03-27), FY2023 (2024-03-28), FY2022 (2023-03-23). $ in thousands unless stated. "Not disclosed" = not in filing.
- FY = Sat closest to Jan 31. FY2025 = 52 wks ended 2026-01-31; FY2024 = 52 wks ended 2025-02-01; FY2023 = 53 wks ended 2024-02-03; FY2022 = 52 wks ended 2023-01-28. Foot Locker (FL) closed 2025-09-08 → FY2025 incl ~21 wks FL.

### 1. Business (Item 1, lines 285-626)
- Overview verbatim (l.289): "DICK'S Sporting Goods, Inc. ... is a leading global sports retailer offering an extensive assortment of authentic, high-quality sports equipment, apparel, footwear and accessories. Our banners include DICK'S Sporting Goods, Golf Galaxy, Public Lands and Going Going Gone! stores in addition to the experiential retail concepts DICK'S House of Sport and Golf Galaxy Performance Center which are all located across the United States. Additionally, as owner and operator of Foot Locker, which includes Foot Locker, Kids Foot Locker, Champs Sports, WSS and atmos banners, we serve the global sneaker community across North America, Europe, Asia and Australia, plus a licensed store presence in Europe, the Middle East and Asia. We also own and operate GameChanger, a youth sports mobile platform for live streaming, scheduling, communications and scorekeeping."
- Founded 1948 Binghamton NY by Richard "Dick" Stack; Edward W. Stack Pres/CEO 1984; renamed DICK'S Sporting Goods, Inc. Apr 1999 (l.291).
- 2 reportable segments from FY2025: DICK'S (DSG, Golf Galaxy, Going Going Gone!, Public Lands, GameChanger) and Foot Locker (FL, Kids FL, Champs, WSS, atmos) (l.293). Employees="teammates"; customers="athletes" (DKS)/"sneakerheads" (FL); FL store employees="stripers"/"blue shirts" (Champs). HQ 345 Court Street, Coraopolis, PA 15108.
- vs FY2024 10-K: FY2024 = single-segment US "omni-channel sporting goods retailer"; FY2025 adds "global", FL banners, 2 segments.

#### 1.2 Store concepts (l.325-329, 413-427)
- DSG store: "unites several sports specialty stores under one roof and typically contains the following specialty shops: Team Sports, Athletic Apparel, Outdoor, Golf, Fitness and Footwear" ("store-within-a-store") (l.417).
- House of Sport (HoS): "provide highly experiential destinations for our athletes. These innovative formats are built around experience, service, community and product" (l.327); HitTrax batting cages, Trackman golf sims, premium full-service footwear decks, House of Cleats.
- Field House: "we are incorporating House of Sport learnings into our most typical 50,000 square foot DICK'S store, which we are calling our DICK'S Field House concept" (l.327).
- Golf Galaxy Performance Center: redesigned GG launched 2021, "equipped with Trackman golf technology and include an elevated staffing and service model".
- Going Going Gone! (GGG): clearance/value; "improve our clearance optimization through the consolidation of clearance inventory for omni-channel selling opportunities to better serve our value athletes" (l.417).
- Public Lands: outdoor; 3 stores at FYE; 1 closure planned for conversion to Field House early FY2026 (l.1392).
- FL Business: "a portfolio of specialty athletic footwear and apparel banners, including Foot Locker, Kids Foot Locker, Champs Sports, WSS and atmos" + licensing w/ 3rd-party operators Middle East/Europe/Asia → royalty income (l.425).
- Fast Break (new FY2025): 11-store FL pilot "which includes enhanced in-store presentation and a more focused assortment"; +10 stores (Los Angeles) early 2026; goal "to have approximately 250 locations ahead of the back-to-school season" 2026 (l.399, 1247, 1605).

#### 1.5 Employees (l.535; FY2024 l.413)
- 2026-01-31: "approximately 31,600 full-time and 73,600 part-time employees, which includes 45,400 who joined our Company as part of the Foot Locker acquisition. Of the total employees for the Foot Locker Business, 34,300 are based in North America and 11,100 internationally".
- 2025-02-01: ~18,600 FT, 37,500 PT; none under collective bargaining.

#### 1.6 GameChanger (l.373)
- "GameChanger, a premier live streaming, scoring and statistics mobile app for youth sports that is offered through a software-as-a-service platform on a subscription basis ... approximately 10 million unique active users on the GameChanger app and generated nearly $150 million in revenue during fiscal 2025."
- Feb 2026 upgrade: HD live streaming, highlight reels, coaching tools; "we expect continued strong revenue growth for GameChanger in 2026". GC subs + DICK'S Media Network (DMN) rev included in digital rev within comps (l.1287). CFO Navdeep Gupta oversees GGG and GC (l.589).

#### 1.7 ScoreCard / DMN (l.387-389)
- "approximately 30 million athletes who participate as members of our ScoreCard Rewards loyalty program, which accounts for over 75% of total sales for the DICK'S Business. Approximately 8 million of the athletes in our loyalty program are part of our ScoreCard Gold tier ... These ScoreCard Gold members account for over 50% of total sales for the DICK'S Business."
- DMN: retail media network monetizing 1P data (in-store/online displays, video, social, email, search, GC app).

#### 1.8 Vertical brands (l.343; RF l.679)
- Owned: Alpine Design, CALIA, DSG, ETHOS, Fitness Gear, MAXFLI, Nishiki, Quest, Tommy Armour, Top-Flite, VRST, Walter Hagen. Licensed: adidas (football), Cobra (golf), Marucci (baseball), Lotto (soccer & pickleball).
- "Our vertical brand products represent approximately 13% of our overall sales within the DICK'S Business" (l.679). (FY2024 same brand list; FY2024 % not re-verified by note author.)
- National brands (l.339): adidas, Asics, Birkenstock, Brooks, Callaway Golf, Carhartt, Columbia, Easton/Rawlings, Hoka, Jordan, New Balance, Nike, On, Patagonia, Peloton, PING, Stanley, TaylorMade, The North Face, Timberland, Titleist, Ugg, Under Armour, Wilson, Yeti.

#### 1.11 eCom / 1.12 Distribution
- Omni: 1-hr in-store/curbside pickup DKS, 2-hr FL, ship-from-store, same-day delivery partners (l.429); eCom % of sales NOT disclosed. Digital rev in comps "includes all eCommerce sales, including omni-channel transactions which are fulfilled by our stores, GameChanger subscriptions as well as revenue from our DICK'S Media Network" (l.1287).
- 13 DCs: 5 regional DKS, 8 FL. New DKS regional DC Fort Worth TX under construction (opening 2026). FL opened leased DC Haps, Netherlands (Sept 2025) replacing Heijen. 9 DCs leased (3 DKS, 6 FL) (l.1573).

### 2. FL acquisition accounting (Note 2 l.2691-2797; Note 5 l.2857-2898)
- Merger Agmt 2025-05-15; FL holders elected "(i) $24.00 in cash or (ii) 0.1168 shares of DICK'S Sporting Goods common stock for each share of Foot Locker common stock"; vote 2025-08-22; election deadline 2025-08-29; closed 2025-09-08 (wholly-owned sub). "Total purchase consideration for the Transaction was $2.5 billion, which was partially funded by cash on-hand".
- 9.6M DKS shares issued (Note 16 l.3712). FL excluded from FY2025 ICFR assessment; = 37% total assets, 18% net sales (l.1839).

- 4.3M FL shs bought for $69.5M in FY2025; non-cash gain $42.2M in other income (Note 12 l.3185).

- Valuation allowances $285.8M at acq (foreign NOLs etc.); $6.4M net DTL to goodwill. Q4 FY2025 measurement-period adj: "$160.4 million increase in goodwill primarily related to the refinement of the fair value of long-lived assets". Not final: "litigation contingencies, long-lived assets, and income taxes".
- "Approximately $24.0 million of the total preliminary goodwill recognized in acquisition accounting is deductible for tax purposes." GW roll-fwd: DKS 245,857 + FL 618,840 − FX (650) = 864,047 at 2026-01-31; DKS GW net of accumulated impairments $115.9M.
- Intangibles: trade names 725,637 (FY2024 15,660; +$710.0M FL trade names incl FX); trademarks 35,165; other indefinite 7,773; total indefinite 768,575; customer lists 18,195 fully amortized; total gross 786,770.
- Acquired gift card liab $22.5M (l.2560). 2029 Notes recorded at FV $384.0M (Note 10).
- Results: "The Foot Locker reportable segment contributed net sales of $3.1 billion and net loss of $60.0 million during fiscal 2025." Segment loss (Note 17) $(52,220)K on sales $3,106,177K.
- Unaudited pro forma (as if closed 2024-02-04), $K exc per sh: net sales FY2025 21,786,502 / FY2024 21,431,183; NI 755,469 / 1,142,955; basic EPS $8.51/$12.69; diluted $8.30/$12.34. FY2024 PF incl $169.5M acq costs; eliminates FL $110M goodwill impairment (26 wks ended 2025-08-02); no synergies; share counts incl 9.6M issued.
- MD&A (l.1315): "Net income also includes $307.3 million, net of tax, or $3.61 per diluted share, of acquisition-related costs". FL PF comps FY2025 "decreased 3.3%", incl Intl −8.1% (l.1235).
- FY2025 acq charges $390.0M: $217.9M inventory write-down/liquidation; $164.2M merger & integration (legal/regulatory, prof services, retention/severance incl $41.7M accelerated equity exp for terminated execs); $7.9M bridge financing. Also $13.4M asset impairment in corp & other.
- "We expect these actions and other merger and integration costs to result in pre-tax charges of $500 million to $750 million, of which $390 million was incurred in fiscal 2025." "We expect approximately $150 million of acquisition-related costs in fiscal 2026."
- "we expect the acquisition to deliver between $100 million to $125 million in cost synergies in the medium-term, to be primarily achieved through procurement and direct sourcing efficiencies."
- Subsequent event (Note 19/Item 9B): 2026-03-23 FL position eliminations + relocation to New York HQ, "approximately $40 - $55 million of charges primarily in the first quarter of 2026".

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Total Senior Notes | | | | 1,900,000 | 1,500,000 | |
| Fin lease oblig (FL) | | | | 39,242 | — | |
| Total debt & fin lease | | | | 1,939,242 | 1,500,000 | |
| Discounts, issuance costs, current fin lease | | | | (33,943) | (15,783) | |
| Total LTD & fin lease | | | | 1,905,299 | 1,484,217 | |
- Exchange Offer: "$381.9 million, or 95.48%, of the Original 2029 Notes were tendered and accepted"; not extinguishment; consents eliminated restrictive covenants + CoC put on $18.1M stub.
- Senior Notes int exp $63.2M FY2025; $55.3M each FY2024, FY2023. Total int exp (IS) $64,263/$52,987/$58,023K (FY25/24/23).
- CoC: repurchase offer at 101%. Covenants: limits on liens, fundamental changes; compliant 2026-01-31. Ratings Moody's Baa2, S&P BBB (l.1595).
- FV vs carrying (Note 12, 2026-01-31, $K): 2029 384,500/394,132; 2032 744,722/690,683; 2052 740,484/548,258. Prior revolver $1.6B dated 2022-01-14, orig maturity 2027-01-14, $500M incremental, SOFR+1.125%.
- Converts history: conversion rate 31.0915 = $32.16 conv price at 2023-01-28 (FY2022 10-K l.2396); $575.0M 3.25% Convertible Senior Notes issued Apr 2020 w/ bond hedge + warrants; remaining $59.1M retired Q1 FY2023, settled substantially in shares; "the Company issued 1.7 million shares of its common stock and recorded $58.5 million to additional paid-in-capital." FY2022 incl exchange of $515.9M aggregate principal.
- New Credit Agmt 2025-06-06 (Wells Fargo admin agent) replaced $1.6B 2022 facility: $2.0B unsecured revolver, $75M LC sublimit, matures 2030-06-06, $700.0M incremental; ABR+0.125% or adj SOFR+1.125% initially (ratings grid); commitment fee 0.11% at 2026-01-31; max lease-adjusted leverage covenant. No borrowings at 2026-01-31/2025-02-01 nor any time FY2025; LCs $26.7M; remaining capacity $1.97B.
- CP program Dec 2025: up to $500.0M, maturities ≤397 days, backstopped by facility; none outstanding.
- Post-FYE (8-K): 2026-09-25 priced $1B senior notes (6.2% due 2036, 6.9% due 2056) — see `DKS_8-K_2026-09-25_1B-senior-notes-offering-6.2pct-2036-and-6.9pct-2056.md`.
- Other commitments: non-cancellable purchase commitments ~$450M (l.1665); UTP liab ~$35.6M incl $4.5M int/penalties (l.3389); deferred comp liab $178.0M (FY2024 $153.7M).

### 4. Leases (Note 8 l.2947-3011)
- Leases "substantially all of its stores, administrative offices for the Foot Locker segment, nine of its distribution centers ... and certain equipment under non-cancellable operating leases that expire at various dates through 2043." DKS stores initial 10-15 yrs w/ multiple 5-yr renewals; FL 5-10 yrs. "Approximately three-quarters of our DICK'S Sporting Goods stores will be up for lease renewal at our option over the next five years" (l.1573).

### 7. Capex & guidance
| FY | Gross capex | Net of constr allowances | Next-yr gdnc (exact) |
|---|---|---|---|
| FY2025 | $1,137.2M (DKS $1,043,463K; FL $93,713K) | $975.5M | "We anticipate fiscal 2026 capital expenditures of approximately $1.5 billion, net of construction allowances provided by landlords." |
| FY2024 | $802.6M | $726.3M | "We anticipate 2025 capital expenditures of approximately $1 billion, net of construction allowances provided by landlords." Plan ~16 HoS 2025, begin construction ~20 for 2026; ~18 Field House, 14 Perf Centers; ~70% relocations/remodels; sqft +~3% |
| FY2023 | $587.4M | $520.4M | FY2024 "approximately $800 million, net": 8 HoS, 10 GG PC, 16 next-gen 50k sqft DSG stores; construction 15 HoS for 2025; new regional DC for 2026 |
| FY2022 | $364.1M | $328.0M | FY2023 "$550 to $600 million, net": 9 HoS, 11 GG PC, 100+ premium footwear decks |
- FY2026 plan (l.1605-1607): ~14 HoS (construction ~18 for 2027), ~22 Field House, ~15 GG PC; "over 75% of our 2026 store openings for the DICK'S Business will be relocations or remodels of existing store locations"; Fort Worth DC 2026; Fast Break ~250 FL locs before BTS; GC/DMN investment.
- FY2026 outlook (l.1253-1265): "we have provided our full year outlook for 2026 and expect total net sales of $22.1 billion to $22.4 billion and earnings per diluted share in the range of $13.70 to $14.70, which includes approximately $150 million of Foot Locker acquisition-related costs anticipated in 2026, offset by income related to litigation and other settlements expected in the first quarter of fiscal 2026." DKS Business: "comparable sales growth for the year to be in the range of 2% to 4% and segment profit to be in the range of $1.58 billion to $1.66 billion, or 11.0% to 11.2% as a percentage of net sales"; slightly higher comps 1H26 (FIFA World Cup); seg margin down 1H, up 2H. FL: "pro forma comparable sales growth to be in the range of 1% to 3% and segment profit to be in the range of $100 to $150 million", weighted 2H26, BTS "the inflection point".
- Liquidity (l.1561): cash $1.4B at 2026-01-31; "We had no revolving credit facility borrowings at any point during 2025."

### 10. Legal (Item 3 l.1121-1141)
1. Securities class action, In re Dick's Sporting Goods, Inc. Securities Litigation, 2:24-cv-00196-NR-KT (W.D. Pa.): filed 2024-02-16 by Plumbers and Pipefitters Local Union No. 719 Pension Trust Fund; lead plaintiffs (2024-07-30) State of Rhode Island Office of the General Treasurer + Western Pennsylvania Teamsters and Employers Pension Fund; consolidated complaint 2024-10-15, §10(b)/20(a), Rule 10b-5 "regarding the Company's inventory, margins, and business prospects, as well as inventory shrinkage related to retail theft"; class period 2022-08-23 to 2023-08-21. MTD filed 2024-12-16; 2025-08-12 Magistrate R&R "recommending that defendants' motion to dismiss be granted in part and denied in part" (shrink, risk factor and some inventory claims recommended dismissed); defendants objected 2025-09-11; co "intends to defend this case vigorously."
2. Derivative: In re Dick's Sporting Goods, Inc. Derivative Litigation, 2:25-cv-00209-NR-KT; actions filed 2025-02-13 and 2025-05-02, consolidated 2025-05-16, stayed pending MTD.
3. §220 demands: letters 2025-06-23, 2025-08-08, 2026-03-18; Anderson v. DICK'S Sporting Goods, Inc., C.A. No. 2025-1269-LM (Del. Ch.) filed 2025-11-03, stayed 2025-11-25 for 4 months or until dismissal decision.
4. Other incidental; no material adverse effect expected.
- vs FY2024: R&R, 2nd derivative/consolidation, §220 matters new. Not in Item 3: Q1 FY2026 interchange-fee litigation settlement (DKS plaintiff) — $150M gain net of legal fees (Note 19), later reported $204.3M received w/ $54.3M allocated to FL purchase price (FY2026 Q1/Q2 10-Qs).

### 11. Cyber (Item 1C l.1011-1041)
- 8-K 2024-08-28 (Item 8.01): "On August 21, 2024, the Company discovered unauthorized third-party access to its information systems, including portions of its systems containing certain confidential information." Response plan, external experts, notified fed law enforcement; "The Company has no knowledge that this incident has disrupted business operations"; "believes that this incident is not material."
- FY2024/FY2025 10-K don't reference incident by date; state "risks from cybersecurity threats, including the results of any previous cybersecurity incidents, have not materially affected the Company... While we have no knowledge of any material data security breaches to date..." FY2024: CISO departed Oct 2024 (CTO interim); new CISO "with over 25 years of experience ... joined the Company in January 2025" (FY2024 l.829).
- FY2025 new: focus FL integration + AI; "the Company temporarily operated under a dual-CISO structure" through Mar 2026; FL systems "continued to operate under their pre-acquisition cybersecurity framework, which may differ from the Company's established standards." Audit Committee oversight; CISO→CTO→CEO; quarterly updates; NIST CSF; IR Plan w/ materiality review.

### 12. Risk factors (Item 1A l.627-995); flags NEW/EXPANDED/UNCHANGED vs FY2024 (FY2025 rewrote nearly all headings: "athletes/teammates"→"customers/employees", "global")
Industry/macro:
1. Macro/discretionary — EXPANDED: geopolitics "Ongoing conflicts and tensions —such as those in the Middle East (particularly the recent U.S. military operations in Iran), Ukraine and Venezuela—... may create economic instability, disrupt supply chains, increase fuel and transportation costs, or elevate cybersecurity risks."
2. Competition — UNCHANGED.
3. Product costs/availability — EXPANDED (tariffs): "Significant increases in those costs—whether due to trade tensions, shifting tariff policies in key sourcing countries like China, Mexico, and Canada (including recently enacted tariffs and potential additional shifts in tariff policies in the future), currency fluctuations, material shortages, supply chain disruptions, or other factors—could increase manufacturing and other costs". FY2024 first added tariffs ("including tariffs recently enacted or that may be enacted in the future by the federal government or by other countries in response to U.S. tariffs"); FY2023/22 none.
4. Foreign manufacturing + intl ops — EXPANDED (intl ops new): "A substantial portion of our merchandise, including most of our vertical brand merchandise, is manufactured abroad ... changes in import duties, quotas, tariffs, and other trade restrictions; the potential introduction or expansion of taxes on imported goods; the loss of favorable trade status; trade remedy actions; geopolitical tensions"; adds foreign indirect taxes, VAT, customs valuation, FX of intl retail ops.
Operations/reputation:
5. Consumer demand/preferences — EXPANDED (adds "fashion trends", FL).
6. Vertical brands/specialty concepts — UNCHANGED; vertical ~13% of DKS Business sales.
7. Reputation — UNCHANGED.
8. Strategic plans may initially hurt results — EXPANDED: Fast Break, AI/ML, DMN, GC.
9. Real estate — EXPANDED (mall exposure new): "Because a meaningful portion of our stores, particularly within our Foot Locker Business, are located in shopping centers or malls, our performance is influenced by the volume of mall traffic and the sustained success and relevance of those locations."; adds store-closure impairment, lease-obligation language.
10. Global distribution/fulfillment network — NEW heading.
11. Data security/privacy — EXPANDED: "cyber threats continue to evolve rapidly accelerated by emerging technologies such as advanced artificial intelligence and machine learning. We may be unable to anticipate, prevent, or fully mitigate new or sophisticated cyberattacks or insider threats"; no cite of Aug 2024 incident.
12. IT systems — UNCHANGED.
13. Employees/organizing — EXPANDED: "whether any unionization or other organizing efforts could occur with our employees within one or more of our domestic or international markets."
14. Key execs — EXPANDED: "including recent hiring and appointments of key executives within our Foot Locker Business."
15. Seasonality/geographic concentration — UNCHANGED (DKS peak Q2/Q4).
16. No guaranty of dividends/buybacks — UNCHANGED.
17. Inventory/shrink — EXPANDED: "our inventory shrink generally stabilized in 2025, we have experienced elevated levels of inventory shrink in the past, which could return or worsen." (shrink RF first FY2023.)
18. FL intl market share incl licensed/franchise — NEW: "Our Foot Locker Business has retail operations in 20 countries across North America, Europe, Asia, and Australia, plus a licensed store presence in Europe, the Middle East, and Asia."
Stock/Class B/anti-takeover:
19. Missing expectations → stock decline — UNCHANGED (first in FY2024 as "We have in the past failed and may in the future fail to meet market expectations...").
20. Class B control — UNCHANGED (majority; % not stated).
21. Class B/anti-takeover — UNCHANGED.
Third parties/legal/regulatory:
22. Key suppliers — EXPANDED (Nike): "We purchased merchandise from approximately 1500 vendors in fiscal 2025, with Nike, our largest vendor, representing approximately 31% of total merchandise purchases. Because of this concentration, any adverse development in a key supplier's brand power, reputation, financial condition, product innovation, or manufacturing capability—or its decision to change its distribution or allocation strategies—could materially adversely affect" business; sub-heading "Any inability to obtain merchandise in a timely manner, secure adequate product allocations, or maintain favorable supplier terms could have a material adverse effect".
23. Global laws/regs — EXPANDED: labor ("increases in minimum wage or overtime obligations, or reforms such as the Protecting the Right to Organize Act"); pandemic language dropped.
24. Product safety/labeling — UNCHANGED. 25. Litigation — UNCHANGED. 26. IP — UNCHANGED.
27. Tax — EXPANDED: foreign audits, digital services taxes, OECD Pillar Two.
28. Pro sports performance/lockouts/strikes/scandals — UNCHANGED.
29. ESG — EXPANDED: SEC, NYSE, "California's Climate Corporate Data Accountability Act and Climate-Related Financial Risk Act, and the European Sustainability Reporting Standards."
Strategic transactions/debt/investments:
30. FL benefits/integration — NEW/materially expanded: "there can be no assurance that we will realize any such synergies or benefits within the anticipated timeframe, or at all."; list: preserving FL relationships; "operating multiple banners, a differentiated store concept and successfully addressing the challenges facing Foot Locker's business"; forecasting/controls; IT/ERP consolidation; "coordinating operations in countries in which we have not previously operated"; retention; "acquisitions may result in dilutive issuances of equity securities, the incurrence of debt, contingent liabilities, amortization expenses, or write-offs, including with respect to historically unproductive inventory in the context of the Transaction".
31. Indebtedness/capital markets — EXPANDED/consolidated (FY2024 had 2 headings): names 2029/2032/2052 notes; "any downgrade—particularly to below investment grade—could increase our interest expense."
32. Value/liquidity of securities & investments — NEW ($50.0M equity-method investment + marketable securities).
- Removed vs FY2024: indenture takeover-provision RF (folded into 31); pandemic/store-closure sentence.
- FY2024 vs FY2023: added tariffs to product-cost & foreign-mfg headings; added missed-expectations RF; split capital availability/indebtedness into 2 headings.
- FY2023 vs FY2022: added shrink RF; teammate RF adds "organizing efforts"; product "labeling"; cost-savings/resourcing in strategic-plans RF; removed 4 convert RFs (hedge/warrant value, counterparty, conversion dilution) + conversion dilution bullet after notes retired Q1 FY2023.
- FY2022: still had convert RFs (3.25% notes due 2025) + separate 2032/2052 indenture takeover RF.

### 13. Other FY2025 10-K items

- SBC incl $41.7M accelerated exp for terminated execs (Note 14 l.3416); 6,267,714 shs available under 2012 Plan.
- Cross-currency swap FV $18.4M (Note 11); AOCI $17,813K at 2026-01-31.
- Equity-method investment $50.0M made FY2025 (Note 12).
- Inventory: DKS weighted avg cost; FL primarily retail inventory method (WSS, atmos weighted avg) (l.1673).

### 0. Key takeaways (compiler)
1. FL lap visible weekly: DKS weekly transaction spend +15% to +58% y/y every week w/e 2025-10-05 → 2026-09-06, then −3.3/−2.4/−3.3 (w/e 13/20/27-Sep-26). FL closed 2025-09-08 → w/e 13-Sep-26 = first lapped week (data DISCLOSED, cause INFERRED).
2. Post-lap DKS beats sub-industry, ~matches broad retail: last-3-wk avg DKS −3.0 vs Sporting Goods Stores −8.3 vs Retail-Disc −3.3 (DERIVED).
3. 2027 Q3 Second Measure (BSM) est 45.97% = flat carry-forward of QTD data mostly pre-lap; PTD est fell 58.31% (day 7) → 45.97% (day 57), should keep falling; cons 21.71% probably incl lap; "Don't read the +24pt gap as a beat" (INFERRED).
4. BSM missed exactly in acquisition qtrs: −16.40pt (2026 Q3), −13.15pt (2026 Q4); within ±3pt every other qtr; slow on step-up → may be slow on step-down (DERIVED/INFERRED).
5. 4 of 5 sources ~46-52% vs 21.7% cons (Similarweb exception) → structural (M&A), not real beat (INFERRED).
6. Facteus may be organic DKS proxy: small post-deal step-up (~9pt vs ~28pt BSM); Aug-26 spend +2.3%, count flat, ticket +2.3% (INFERRED).

### 7. Anomalies / data-quality flags
- w/e 28-Dec-25: DKS +57.0, SG +27.8, RD +8.3, spike across every category → holiday/calendar alignment, not DKS-specific (INFERRED).
- w/e 14-Jun/21-Jun-26: SG −25.4→+29.4; DKS +2.5→+58.4 → Father's Day shift (6/15/25 vs 6/21/26); average the 2 wks (INFERRED).
- w/e 01-Mar-26 Electronics +88.7 one-off outlier; ignore.
- QTD cons −5.24/0.30/0.23% scattered days = glitch; Diff meaningless.
- BSM vs Facteus Aug +22.3 vs +2.3 → likely Facteus excludes FL banners (small post-deal step-up, §9b) (INFERRED).
- Adj vs raw: QTD Adj ~46-52%; raw weekly/monthly ~18-29% pre-lap; different bases.
- Brands = All Covered incl FL banners → total-company spend vs DKS-only comps mismatch.

## SRC 00_DKS_SYNTHESIS/DKS_Conference_and_Event_Transcript_Highlights.md | compiled 2026-10-05 | synthesis of 7 FactSet sell-side conf transcripts + FL acquisition call + 2026 AGM | 2024-09 → 2026-09
- Quotes verbatim from FactSet (incl artifacts "[ph]", "[indiscernible]"); no editorial opinion in source.

### 1. Event index

- FL call Q&A analysts: Brian Nagel (Oppenheimer), Paul Lejuez (Citi), Adrienne Yih (Barclays), Simeon Gutman (MS), Robby Ohmes (BofA), Kate McShane (GS), Michael Lasser (UBS), Chris Horvers (JPM), Mike Baker (D.A. Davidson).

### 2. FL acquisition call 2025-05-15
#### Deal terms (Gupta)
- "DICK'S will acquire Foot Locker for $24 per share." "Foot Locker shareholders will have the option to receive either $24 in cash or 0.1168 shares of DICK'S common stock. The election is not subject to a minimum or maximum amount of cash or stock consideration."
- "This transaction implies an equity value of approximately $2.4 billion and an enterprise value of $2.5 billion."
- Financing (to Yih): "as we guided today, our intention is to use a combination of cash, long term debt, as well as equity as we look to finance this transaction."; "We finished last year with $1.7 billion of cash on the balance sheet."; "even if these were to be fully financed using the debt, it won't impact unfavorably our credit ratings today and our intention is to continue to maintain a strong investment grade credit rating on a go forward basis as well."
- FL bonds: "we are working with our advisors to see if there is an opportunity to exchange the obligations and if need be we'll be able to take those notes out as well."
- "the deal has been unanimously approved by both DICK'S and Foot Locker's board of directors, and while we believe it is expected to close in the second half of 2025, it is subject to customary closing conditions, including the Regulatory and Foot Locker shareholder approval."
- Regulatory (Hobart to Baker): "we are not expecting any regulatory concerns. Obviously, we're going to go through the process and see where we net out, but we're not expecting that to be too cumbersome."
#### Synergies/accretion (Gupta)
- "We expect to deliver between $100 million to $125 million in cost synergies in the medium-term, primarily from procurement and direct sourcing efficiencies. We believe these synergies are well-supported and achievable."
- "We also expect the deal to be accretive to EPS in the first full fiscal year post-close excluding one-time transaction costs and costs to achieve these synergies."
- Composition (to Lejuez): "we feel there is opportunity both on the gross margin line and getting the scale of the two companies, bringing them together, as well as there are opportunities on the SG&A line when you think about the procurement opportunities that we see across the company."
- Scope (to Baker): "we are talking about both because you have opportunity both on the procurement vendor, the direct sourcing vendors as well as the non-merge vendors." (merch + non-merch procurement)
- Hobart: "the opportunities that Navdeep is talking about are really things like procurement and purchasing and things like that, those are the synergies that we are counting on at this point."
- Stack (to Lasser) comp/EPS assumptions withheld: "We have a set of assumptions that we're not ready to make public. But as we go through this transition between now and close, when we close, we'll provide what we think this – what the comp is going to be, what we think the EPS is going to be, a better view of what the accretion is going to be."
#### Prelim Q1 FY2025 (same morning, Gupta)
- "Our first quarter comps grew 4.5% and we delivered a non-GAAP EPS of $3.37 ahead of last year." Full Q1 results 2025-05-28.
#### FL profile (Stack prepared)
- "With Foot Locker, we have the opportunity to expand our business internationally, which bring our total store count to more than 3,200 in key regions, and open the door to an approximately $300 billion global sports retail market." US TAM: "in the US alone, the total addressable market across footwear, apparel and hardlines is approximately $140 billion."
- Banners FL, Kids FL, Champs, WSS, atmos. "With approximately 2,400 stores across 26 countries at the end of 2024, including key markets in North America, Europe, Asia, Australia and New Zealand, and a licensed store presence in Europe, the Middle East and Asia." "completing over 400 store refreshes last year, and growing digital sales to 18% of total revenue." "In 2024, Foot Locker generated $8 billion in revenue."
- Gupta (to Yih): "the footwear business as a combined company would be close to about 50% of the total sales of the company."
#### Rationale
- Hobart 5 benefits (slide 6): "First, the combination is focused on creating a global platform within the growing sports retail industry. Second, it'll allow us to serve a broader set of consumers across differentiated concepts. Third, it will allow us to strengthen relationships with brand partners through global reach. Fourth, we will invest in future growth through an industry leading omni-channel experience. And fifth, we will unlock operational efficiencies to create shareholder value. Overall. This is a growth oriented transaction grounded in operational discipline."
- Stack: "The convergence of sport and culture has never been stronger and we're seeing tremendous momentum and opportunity across our industry." Precedent: "we have successfully acquired and integrated major brands before like Galyan's, which was similarly sized acquisition relative to the scale of our business when we acquired them."
- Stack: "Foot Locker has a consumer – it serves a consumer in neighborhood that we in many cases are never going to get. They're in places that we're not going to be able to find 50,000 square feet or 60,000 square feet or [ph] address (00:27:49) that we could – that would give us the return."
- Hobart: "Here in the US, Foot Locker has a real estate portfolio that is highly complementary to ours, with stores in different markets that serve different needs and demographics from DICK'S."; "Foot Locker enables us to reach more sneakerheads and lifestyle focused shoppers, a great addition to the performance focused consumers who are key customers for us."; "the TAM that we're operating in is growing from $140 billion to $300 billion. That's because of the global reach."
- Stack: "we've always talked about footwear as the engine that pulls this train."; "with the World Cup coming in 2026, Olympics in 2028, the World Cup is going to be the biggest sporting event this country has ever seen."
- Stack: "two iconic brands will create a new global leader in the sports retail industry."; "We're doing this from a position of strength and we think this can only improve what Foot Locker is doing and what we're doing with DICK'S is not going to change. That glide path is still there."; "we knew there would be some skepticism, but we're up for the jab and we're highly confident."
#### Nike (Stack)
- "Foot Locker was really, a big part of Foot Locker under pressure was what the relationship they had with Nike. Nike moved into DTC and away from wholesale partners a while back, hurt Foot Locker pretty significantly, Nike's changed their position really leaning back into wholesale. I think Elliott and his team are doing a great job and we're pretty excited about what's going on with Nike." "Foot Locker is going to be a beneficiary of that move back to from a wholesale standpoint. And so, we think the timing of this is perfect." "We couldn't be more excited to lean into Nike."
- "We've talked with Nike, Foot Locker is a very important part of Nike's long term strategy and we're happy to have Foot Locker and us together, take advantage of all those things that are going to come down the pipe with Nike and some of the other key brands that we do business with."
- Nike mix at FL (to Ohmes): "I think what's going to happen is that the Nike business will probably go up. We expect the Nike business will go up at a higher margin rate because they will be able to sell more product at full price, as they weren't getting as much product that could be sold at full price as they did in the past. And that's a big precept of this."
- "From talking with other – some of the brands on this and we have talked to a couple of them, they're pretty enthusiastic about this."
- Distribution risk (to Gutman): "everything that we've talked to them about, everything that we've seen is that they're not looking to change their distribution strategy and broaden it significantly where access to some of this high heat product would go to other places that it doesn't have today... The brands have indicated they're going to continue to lean into specialty and make sure that their product is positioned in a premium way."
#### Integration/governance
- Hobart: "It's really important to know that we plan to run these two businesses separately... Similarly, the Foot Locker team will be focused exclusively on running the Foot Locker business, and we will have a team here, a small team here led by Ed that will help with that integration and just sharing some of that knowledge and expertise and executional excellence that Ed mentioned."
- Banners: "For now, because we are leading the two companies, we're going to keep them separate. The consumer may or may not know that DICK'S and Foot Locker are one."
- Closures (to Ohmes): "They've indicated there are some that are potentially being closed, but that's something that we're going to get involved with in the next several months and have more of an opinion. It's not our expectation that we'll be closing a significant number of Foot Locker stores. We are here to reinvigorate the Foot Locker brand."
- Hobart: "it's really critical to know that nothing is changing, with our growth rate, our expectations for House of Sport, for our Field House concepts."
- Stack board precept: "the board was very adamant about with us, with their direction, is that when we do this, it cannot impact what we're doing from DICK'S, it can impact what we're doing from a House of Sport, Field House, what we're doing from GameChanger, investing from a technology standpoint, and the flywheel that we've got going with DICK's and strong DICK's is."
- Intl (to Horvers): Hobart "DICK'S is staying on its current growth trajectory and current strategies, which don't include, at this time, international expansion."; "who knows about the future"; Stack "So we have no plans to take DICK'S internationally."
- FL apparel (Stack to Lasser): "They've talked that their apparel business is deteriorating. They haven't invested in that in bringing apparel back for this consumer. This kid dresses from toe to head."
#### Q&A
1. Nagel (Oppenheimer) why now/brands: Stack "This is something we've looked at for and have thought about it for a long time."; "the brands are really very excited about this transaction, and are willing to help and are going to be very helpful with this transaction."
2. Lejuez (Citi) synergy timing/SG&A/banners: Gupta "$100 million to $125 million of synergy over the medium term. And we'll provide more color as we are closer to the close of the transaction." Hobart: run separately; synergies = procurement/purchasing; praised Lace Up plan: "reinventing stores, the reimagined store, the refreshed stores, investing in tag, investing in personalization and just all of the consumer experience, investing in the Stripers."
3. Yih (Barclays) pre-deal Nike talks/TAM/footwear share/leverage: Stack "yes, we talked with several brands about the opportunity for us to do this... They gave us a surrogate impression of what was going on with Foot Locker."; "part of our job is to be able to see around the corners and see what's coming next." Gupta footwear ~50%; $1.7B cash; cash+LTD+equity; IG maintained.
4. Gutman (MS) why buy shrinking comp business; 5-yr distribution protection: Stack "Foot Locker has been shrinking, but still – I hope everybody can remember what happened with Nike's change in their distribution strategy... Foot Locker bore the brunt of that change."; "almost everybody knows we're pretty conservative. We don't get over skis."; "We think this makes us much bigger, much more important partners to those brands." Hobart: "this obviously widens the addressable market for us significantly."
5. Ohmes (BofA) Nike % FL post-merger; HoS; rightsizing: Nike "will probably go up... at a higher margin rate"; no change DKS growth; no significant FL closures.
6. McShane (GS) tariffs: Stack "tariffs are something we think about all the time... There was some good news that came out of Geneva last week, that is positive. But we've been able to manage through tariffs in the past. We'll manage through these tariffs again."
7. Lasser (UBS) vs failed horizontal mergers; FL comp assumption: Stack "We think the big difference here is that footwear is part of our core competence. We know this business extremely well... we've got visibility for Foot Locker performing meaningfully better."; "We don't have a lot of big egos here in trying to do something just to do it. We're very conservative. And if we didn't see this clear line of sight to this or we thought that this was going to impact what we're able to do with DICK's, we wouldn't be doing it." Comp assumption not disclosed until close.
8. Horvers (JPM) intl/crossover: Hobart no intl for DKS now; crossover "our market share in this $140 billion market that we've been competing in thus far is still around 80% [sic — transcript; elsewhere mgmt cites ~8.5%–9%]. So there's an enormous market share... with our 90% of our stores having premium full service footwear decks... So I don't see these two things as being cannibalistic."
9. Baker (D.A. Davidson) Golf Galaxy analogy/FTC/procurement: Stack "it strengthened our partnership with those golf brands, which has really helped us not only in Golf Galaxy but DICK's going forward. And this is pretty similar to that... I think it's a very good analogy."; Hobart no regulatory concerns; Gupta merch + non-merch procurement.
- Close (Stack): "We're very excited about this acquisition."

### 3.1 GS conf 2024-09-05 (Hobart, Gupta)
- New: first conf post Q2 FY24 (reported prior day): comp +4.5% on +2%, gdnc raised; GameChanger (GC) first dedicated airtime (users, rev, CAGR, engagement, 2x ScoreCard overlap); "vertical brands" replaces "private label"; HoS cadence (12 → ~20 → +15 in 2025; 75-100 by 2027); Field House named "internal name for the next-gen 50K square foot format store"; return to positive sq ft growth; new DC 2026.
- Hobart: Q2 comp "4.5% on top of a 2% the year prior"; "1.6 million new athletes"; "growth across every income demographic"; "didn't see trade down."
- Gupta: TAM "$140 billion industry"; share "[ph] just under (04:40) 8.5% share today"; industry "pre-pandemic was growing probably in [ph] low (05:19) single-digits"; last ~5 yrs "growing at around 6% CAGR"; "Three-fourth of the US population today is participating in an active lifestyle. There are 27 million kids that are active under the age of 17 that are active in youth sports."
- Hobart: "Over 70% of our sales go through a loyalty [indiscernible] (10:07) ScoreCard program"; program "for 20 years at least."
- Gupta: "There are 6 million athletes on the GameChanger platform"; GC athletes who are ScoreCard members: "Their revenue is two times higher than an average ScoreCard user." Hobart: GC users "spending 45 minutes a day"; "We've said it's a $100 million in revenue this year. It's highly profitable Software as a Subscription. It's a freemium model." Gupta: GC "growth that we have seen in the recent five years is about 35% to 40% CAGR."
- Gupta: vertical brands "outperformed the company average and these brands carry 600 to 800 basis points of higher margin rate"; Q2 "90 basis points of merch margin expansion."
- HoS: Hobart "over 100,000 square feet", "75 to 100" target; Field House "50,000." Gupta: "we finished last year with 12 House of Sport location. Our goal this year is to open eight. We have opened two this year"; "approximately about 15 locations as we see opening – being opened in 2025"; "We'll finish with around 20 locations by the end of this year"; site "100,000 to 120,000 square foot"; "75 to 100 locations by 2027"; "we see us continuing to march towards that 75 to 100 locations by 2027."
- Gupta: Field House "we will open about 11 of those this year and that will reach around 26"; "we don't own any of our stores"; "On an average, we have about 100 stores that come up for lease renewal each year." GG Perf Center goal "about 45 to 50" (TrackMan). GGG "about 50 stores." "We return to positive square footage growth this year. We are expecting our square footage to increase by about 2%"; "new distribution center that will be opening in 2026."
- Rapid-fire: back half Hobart "We've seen strong momentum with our consumer and we expect that to continue into the back half"; costs Gupta "will moderate... but probably we'll see some pressure, especially on the labor side"; "tariff is still unknown"; holiday promo Hobart "I don't expect it to be materially different."
- Quotes: Hobart "from 2016 on, we say nothing about our business is the same, absolutely nothing."; "sport and culture have come together now in such a way that our House of Sport stores, for example, are [ph] just dead (07:27) right in the center of a sport and culture and lifestyle and performance."; "Our merchant team has done an absolutely incredible job, getting – making partnerships with strategic, our vendor partners. In fact, those relationships are at an all-time high."; "many of our preferred partners, I think are realizing the benefits of wholesale and that's a positive for us."; "We don't call them private labels anymore. We call it vertical brands."; GC "It's incredibly addictive... that's like TikTok levels of engagement."; "mall developers and landlords who are noticing when they put in a House of Sport, they're seeing increased traffic to their malls, increased sales per square foot, and that's getting us access to a whole different real estate base."; "it is the best expression of the DICK'S brand, but it is translating into our 50,000 Field House concept as well."; "access will continue to be a really important part of our story". Gupta "we don't want to have a tired old chain."; "these stores can work really well in a small market to a mid-market as well as in a really urban location like Prudential Center." Both: "Relentless improvement."

### 3.2 MS conf 2024-12-03 (Hobart, Gupta)
- New: post Q3 FY24 (comp 4.2%; 3rd straight 4%+ qtr; 3rd consecutive raise); vs-2019 framing sales >50% higher, profit ~4x; Nike 24%; 80% multi-brand shoppers; 90% stores premium full-service decks; inventory +13% defended as "strategic weapon"; value chain (50 stores) liquidation outlet; GC $30-40B TAM, RMN ambition, "Yes and yes" on 2025 ad rev & data use; capital "optimally levered", "lean in and support the valuation" on dislocation.

- Hobart Nike "24%"; "80% of our shoppers are multi-brand shoppers"; "90% of our stores have premium full service decks." "They are our number one strategic partners, but only 24% of the business."; "we're very excited about what we're seeing coming down the pike with Nike, and they're running construct."; "it's not just a two brand story"; "tremendous long term potential".

Returns: "The financial returns we have posted that, you can see that on our Investor Relations website, and those are just the returns that we see in year one... At the beginning of this year, we had only two stores that had comped fully in the second year. Now, we have more stores." (Gutman framing "three years for a House of Sport, and two years for a Field House" payback not confirmed/corrected.)
- Hobart "our Q3 was up 13%" (inventory); "I would think of it as a strategic weapon and asset that we have." Gupta "this is also a short holiday season. We have five less days compared to last year."; "Now, we have 50 stores that can actually handle all of – if there is a mistake" (value chain).
- Hobart vertical "600 basis points to 800 basis points higher margin rate"; "collectively, our vertical brands are our number two vendor only behind Nike"; "We have $2 billion is the goal that we have set."
- Gupta GC industry "$30 billion to $40 billion in size"; "Our revenue is $100 million by the end of this year"; growth "around 30% to 40% each year"; basketball AI launched; volleyball tool.
- Hobart pre-COVID: "in the quarters leading up to COVID, we were 5% or 6% comp. And right in the weeks before COVID, we were up 6% or 7% comp."; "we have 50,000 unbelievable teammates." Gupta "we have the best data set in our sporting goods industry."; "We would try and finish at the end of the year with no debt on the balance sheet."
- Quotes: Hobart "we're well past looking backwards at COVID, and just looking at our future and how we're going to continue to grow."; "sport is having an outsized influence on culture and culture is having an outsized influence on sport."; HoS "They're driving longer to come to a House of Sport. Their travel time is more. They're spending more time in a House of Sport."; "the ON came in through Public Lands in a House of Sport, and FP Movement came in through a House of Sport."; "It is at the center point of how we will reposition our entire portfolio."; "we want to become the best sports company in the world. That is our vision and the best sports company in the world would own the best sports tech platform in the world, and GameChanger is that."; "Our consumer has been holding up very well, and that's been the case for multiple quarters now."; "we don't have to get distracted by a level of a frenzy of promotional activity because we have product that doesn't need to be discounted." Gupta "We no longer have rooted our brand into one particular brand, even in footwear decks." GC ad rev & data use 2025: "Yes and yes." Hobart "it's the start of a long-term journey. So, it's not going to be remotely done in 2025. It will be early innings." Gupta "Well, we are a conservative company." / "if there is a dislocation in the marketplace, we will definitely lean in and support the valuation of the company."

- New: 2025 plan 15 HoS + 20 Field House; tariff "2018 and 2019 playbook"; vertical brands $1.6B last yr vs $2B goal; RMN "just launched"; capex "up to $800 million this year"; 100% FCF returned last 3 yrs; Texas (50 stores; Katy & Baybrook conversions; DC "hole in the middle of America"); RFID 2 yrs in; Ross Park cannibalization example.

We doubled our dividend in 2023... we have paid dividends for over 10 years now"; "our CapEx profile, it's up to $800 million this year." Hobart: Nike "our largest vendor but they are only 24%"; "80% of our shoppers come in and want a multi-branded experience." Some people are concerned about going into wholesale. We have all the time in the world to get people to understand how we can bring our brand to life."; "we always say that footwear is the engine that pulls the train."; moat "I feel like the secret sauce behind our success that is not really well-known is the quality and the caliber of the team that we have, the 50,000 teammates."; competition "There isn't one national competitor that we'd say, that's the one. But it keeps us on our toes because in any given category, competing with somebody who might just focus on that one category, it's a challenge for us."; "We are competing with the best specialty."; HoS origin "that was Ed's challenge when he gave it to the team and said, I want you to come up with the store. If it opened across the street from us, it would kill DICK's Sporting Goods."; Nike history "the kids would come in and say, okay, this is fantastic, but where's the cool Nike shoe that I need, and we didn't have it."; "So almost definitionally, the consumer has been a little better than we even have predicted."; "our vision is a lofty vision. We want to be the best sports company in the world." Hobart "it's the sixth quarter in a row, we've had over 4% comps"; "growth across every income demographic"; "We're not seeing trade down."; "we're number one sporting goods retailer in the US, soon to be in the globe, but we only have 9% market share."
- Gupta: "in Q2, we finished with 7% growth in inventory... 7% growth in inventory and 5% sales growth"; clearance "at historic lows"; "we expect our inventory growth to continue to moderate as we go into the second half."; "there was negligible impact from the tariff, but as I expected, as every other retailer has said, that this will grow as we look to the back half."; GGG "50 stores today"; "in Q2, we grow our gross margins by 30 basis points and that was on top of over 200 basis points of gross margin."
- Stack vertical "margin rates there, 700 basis points to 900 basis points higher."; Betts bats "$500 a piece"; Maxfli "won 13 majors over 100 tournaments"; "Ben won twice [ph] On Tour."; golf "is our third largest department."

Our plan for next year is approximately same, [ph] current (00:40:41) 15 to 16 House of Sport openings next year, and our long-term aspirations till 2027 that we have shared is to get to 75 to 100."; "Within the core DICK'S, we think that the unit growth will be relatively flattish, but you will see the square footage increase."; "all the new store openings or relocations or conversions will be to the Field House format."

- Stack hunt exit: "That whole outdoor business was roughly $1 billion in business"; "the margin rates were roughly 1,700 basis points lower in that aspect of the business than the company average"; "if we can recapture roughly 60% of the sales, we'll be fine... We tested 10 stores and what we found is we didn't capture 60%. We captured 105%... We did another 25 stores."

Things that have been around for a while are a little bit slower."; FL "it's really basically into [ph] retail 101 (00:18:54) to have kind of the right product in at the right place at the right time."; "The striper culture in the stores is one of the main reasons why we bought them. These young men and women who work in the stores, they love Foot Locker. They love sneakers. They understand sneakers."; "the next couple of quarters are going to be – we're going to kind of clean things up, get real assessment of what's going on, and we can give you a lot more information on our fourth quarter call as we go into 2026."; World Cup "the way that the World Cup has been structured, I think is just genius, that it's all of North America."; vertical ceiling "it'll grow a bit as a percentage, but I don't think it'll ever get to be super high because our footwear business is such an important part of that business, and we're not going to be in the vertical brand footwear business."; "Nike's new running construct, which has been just fantastic around Peg, the structure, and Vomero has been great."; "the whole precept of is we need to build the concept that will kill DICK'S Sporting Goods."; "we'll have another conversation 10 years from now that we've changed a whole lot 10 years in the future from what we do today." The vast majority of our portfolio is 50,000 to 60,000 boxes." Hobart "we've created a much better mousetrap and we're ready to go. So, we've got a sales force now."; "So, we're bullish. That's why we took our guidance up."

GC 9M unique users; $40B youth sports tech; flag football added; Unrivaled investment; $100M incl media. Gymshark in 12 HoS (first US retailer); Collectors Clubhouse; World Cup balls. Agentic commerce/AI framework (3 buckets); ~90% of products ship from stores; 800-plus stores. 2026 new DC flagged as SG&A investment.
- Stack HoS: "since 2022, when we opened up the first one, to now we've got 35 of these"; "We've opened up 15 of them this year, we'll open up roughly 15 of them next year"; lab "about 25,000 square feet down in the basement"; concept began "close to 10 years ago", restarted "seven years ago". A-mall "high-performing malls at $800 to $1,200, $1,500 a square foot."
- Stack FL: "Somewhere between, however you calculate it, 500 to 600 basis points of margin erosion. And we think we can we can reverse that trend"; "There's roughly 30% less SKUs in these stores that we've redone"; test began "literally on like day 10"; "I spend every other week here in New York in Foot Locker's offices."; Q4 FL "expect our margin rates to be 1,000 to 1,500 basis points below what Foot Locker was last year"; "Our comps are going to be down mid-single digits to high-single digits."; "we're cleaning out the garage, we're getting rid of all of this product that we – that shouldn't have, that's old, that hasn't been – hasn't selling."; "We've got a lot of work to do. So, the solution is simple, the work is hard."
- Hobart: "We've had seven quarters in a row now with a comp over 4%. We came off a 5.7% comp in Q3, which two year's back is 10%"; "we just took our guidance up for the – for Q4."; "We don't comment on inter-quarter but thank you for asking."; "newness is a theme".

- Stack "We've got Gymshark in the – in 12 House of Sport stores right now."; "We've got Gymshark and are the first retailer to have Gymshark in the US."; "I think there's a lot of opportunity ahead with Nike."; Nike running construct "out of this world".
- GC: Hobart "we have 9 million unique users"; Gupta "$40 billion is the youth sports tech industry"; "$100 billion of revenue last year, continuing $100 million of revenue last year [sic]" (=$100M) "and very profitable"; growth "30% to 40%"; "basketball, volleyball, flag football are the new sports"; avg session "about 45 minutes"; "$100 million that we have talked about includes the media."; "we are in middle stages of our DICK'S Media Network and the GameChanger capability." Stack "We led the last round of funding for Unrivaled."
- Hobart "our 800-plus stores"; "almost 90% of our products come out of our stores." Gupta "2026, we'll opening a new distribution center."
- Quotes: Stack Nike at first HoS "Nike came in... they said, this is the best expression of sport any place in the world. The question is, can you really scale it?"; "the most misunderstood aspect of this is how simple this turnaround is. And not that it's not hard work, but we know exactly what we need to do, we know exactly how we need to do it, we know exactly who's going to do it, we knew exactly who's going to partner with us on it. And this is basically back to retail 101."; "All these brands need a stable, growing, viable, predictable Foot Locker that they haven't had in the past, and they are all in in helping us helping us turn this around."; "our team did not buy the product for Q1 and Q2... the first time that we were able to touch the – and build the assortment for what Foot Locker's going to look like is in the back-to-school time. And that's why we say that you'll see a real step change in back-to-school."; FL wall "if you walked into a Foot Locker store and looked at the wall, it was merely a run-on sentence, just a bunch of shoes stuck on the wall."; "We don't want to have it death by 1,000 cuts."; Manhattan HoS "To put a House of Sport in Manhattan, I won't say it's never going to happen, but I can't imagine it ever happening. The rents are just so high, the economics don't work, and we're not ever going to build these stores that it's okay to lose $5 million, we're going to chalk it off to marketing."; EMEA "we're keeping that, so we're not we're not getting rid of that"; "North America is ahead of EMEA in this turnaround, but it will get there."; "It's also given us an opportunity to be in some of the most iconic real estate in the country that, five years ago, we wouldn't get access to."; "there's a five-, six-, seven-year window here that sports are going to be even more central to this country than they are today." Hobart "that's enabled the DICK'S team to stay completely focused on the DICK'S flywheel, which is one of the absolute prerequisites of doing this deal."; GC "it's its eyeballs of youth sports that can't be acquired anywhere else in the marketplace."; AI "we have a differentiated assortment of products, which is going to be, I think, a key driver in an AI-enabled world, an agentic commerce-enabled world." Gupta "the rubric does not change. We will drive – our focus is going to be continuing to drive the top line and the bottom line improvement."

### 3.6 JPM forum 2026-04-08 (Stack, Hobart, Gupta)
- New: Fast Break named/quantified 11 → 21 stores (10 added around NBA All-Star in LA) → ~250 US stores by BTS 2026; Europe started "roughly six months behind"; "capital-light"; House of Hoops walls removed. "If we had a mulligan, we'd buy this all over again"; "not a defensive play". FL margin "won't say that we're going to get that back in year one, but you're going to start to see margin rate improvement"; "nothing structural". 2026 DKS comp plan 2-4%; Q4 FY25 comp 3.1% on 6.6% (2-yr ~10%); traffic down in Q4. HoS target reset 67 by end-2027 vs 75-100 — attributed to patience for A-mall real estate; capex $1.5B 2026; "just over 30" HoS in 2026 capex incl 18 opening 2027. Vertical premium "even becoming better" than 700-900bps. GC ~$150M rev; ~40% CAGR 3 yrs; Bat Lab; combined DMN go-to-market. Engagement scores ~2,000bps above retail avg; FL culture "somewhat siloed". Synergy $100-125M cost only; broader "access, allocation"; Nike sponsoring DKS ad. KFL kids' basketball underinvested. Dividend raised 12 straight yrs; buybacks offset dilution; sales ~60% above 2019 on same DC count.
- Stack: "We've only got roughly 9% market share here in the US."; Fast Break "We took roughly 30% of the SKUs out"; "Around the NBA All-Star Game, we went – we did 10 more of these stores in LA"; "we'll have around 250 of these stores in the US by back to school"; "It's up to 21 now"; FL "a store of like 650 or 700 stores."; FL "margin rates were down 500 basis points to 600 basis points."; Europe "a couple of Fast Break stores in Europe"; "roughly six months behind what we do in the US."; "we plan to Dick's to 2% to 4% comp growth."
- Gupta: Q4 "We posted a 3.1% comp on top of a 6.6% comp from the year prior. So on a two-year stack basis, if you look at it, we were almost about 10% comp"; World Cup "contemplated into the guidance."
- Horvers (analyst framing): "it looks like you're about 22% of Nike's wholesale business"; DKS advertising "4% of sales"; "67 House of Sports by the end of 2027" vs "initial 75 to 100"; "CapEx going to $1.5 billion in this year" (accepted without correction).
- Gupta synergy "$100 million to $125 million over the medium term"; "two components: one, merchandising synergies and the non-merchandising buying."
- Stack: "this new access to real estate is what the difference is between the 75 and the 68. And when you take 67 or 68 and when you look at that, it's a pretty meaningless difference in the grand scheme of things." Gupta: 2026 capex includes "about close to just over 30 House of Sport store locations, including 18 that we'll be opening in 2027."
- Gupta vertical "We have said in the recent years that it's now 700 basis point to 900 basis point. Actually, the margins are even becoming better than the – our current expectations."
- GC: Gupta "almost about $150 million in revenue size"; "probably one of the most not well understood and a true hidden gem is the GameChanger business"; "the 40% CAGR that it has had for the last several years"; Hobart "We've had about a 40% CAGR three years"; "GameChanger because of the authenticity in Diamond Sports has created a concept called Bat Lab."
- Stack engagement survey "almost 2,000 basis points higher than the average retail."; KFL "probably talked to over 100 different managers... there was a common theme around kids, and it was kids' basketball, kids' basketball, kids' basketball."; GGG "the footwear capacity in the Going Going Gone stores has never been at 100%."
- Gupta: "our sales are up over 60% – or close to 60% versus 2019. We have the same exact number of distribution center"; dividends grown "12 straight years"; buyback "just basically offsetting the normal dilution. But we'll continue to be opportunistic with the share buyback. We have plenty of capacity on the balance sheet."; "our first priority is to invest into the business."; "We have not given a long-term rubric, and I won't do that today."
- Quotes: Stack "if we had a mulligan, we'd buy this all over again, and we would do nothing different."; "This was not a defensive play... I'm not sure I would characterize the brands as irrational, or at least I wouldn't say that publicly."; "since we closed, we're even more excited about the opportunity than we were when we first bought the business."; "there's been no pushback from the vendors at all."; "the brands that have kind of taken some allocation away or some access away have come back and said, we're back in."; lease line "The lease line is that billboard with the consumers walking by. You've got to get that lease line and make it interesting for them to come into the store."; "I won't say that we're going to get that back in year one, but you're going to start to see margin rate improvement."; "I don't think there's anything structural. We're not going to give a timeframe right now, but there's nothing structural that would keep you from being able to do that."; "it'll be that this was a transformational acquisition that Dick's made for Foot Locker – with Foot Locker."; "Foot Locker in the past was too dependent on that high-heat product... We're going to build out this base."; "in Europe, they didn't buy a retro run shoe, they didn't buy a P-6000, they didn't buy a Merrell 5, they didn't buy anything from a retro run standpoint. And that's a big miss."; "They had too much of the wrong product and not enough of the right product."; HoS pace "We don't want to rush. We don't want to go and hit a number to just be able to hit a number."; "The volume of these stores that we're going to do are going to be meaningfully – we expect to be meaningfully higher than what we had originally anticipated."; A-mall "don't automatically go to the fact that the rents are going to be higher."; FL culture "If you sat and talked to people in Foot Locker today and said, how are things today versus what they were six, eight months ago? They would tell you it's entirely different."; "we're not going to let that run-on sentence come back in in Foot Locker."; "We will be growing footwear"; "We still have upside across". Hobart Fast Break "they're really capital-light... usually not swinging hammers. It's just – so it's really more of a visual merchandise update which is low risk and high reward."; culture "It is a secret asset. It is a secret weapon."; "the Media Network actually goes to market as one where you can have access to all things sport, our scorecard data, as well as our GameChanger data, as well as our teammate product knowledge."; "we haven't seen trade down from best to better and better to good." Gupta "we don't call core non-core. We see Dick's and Footlocker as the core parts of the business."; "Those investments show up in SG&A, but the benefits show up in margin."; "actually Nike is sponsoring that ad with us. They gave us access to their athletes."

### 3.7 AGM 2026-06-10 (procedural; no operating commentary)
- Record date 2026-04-13; notices mailed from 2026-05-01; Secretary: "We have received proxies representing more than 93% of the eligible votes for this meeting."
- Proposals: (1) elect 11 directors (Mark J Barrenechea, Emmanuel Chirico, William J Colombo, Robert W. Eddy, Anne Fink, Larry Fitzgerald Jr, Lauren R. Hobart, Sandeep Mathrani, Desiree Ralls-Morrison, Lawrence J Schorr, Edward W Stack); (2) say-on-pay; (3) ratify Deloitte & Touche FY2026; (4) stockholder proposal (Curtis Hill, Free Enterprise Project of the National Center for Public Policy Research) report on "business risk assessment and decision-making framework as it relates to women's rights."
- Prelim: all directors elected; say-on-pay approved; auditor ratified; "stockholders did not approve Proposal 4." Final via 8-K within 4 business days; board response to Prop 4 at proxy p.61.

### 3.8 GS conf 2026-09-14 (Stack, Hobart, Gupta)
- Context: first conf after gdnc cut; McShane: "the market was surprised by the cut, both on the DICK'S core margins and on the Foot Locker comp and margins". Mgmt: "athletic cycle is over" narrative "overdone"; issue = legacy silhouettes, launch/retro weakness, aggressive brand discounting, supply constraints on new product — "Don't see this as a demand issue whatsoever."
- Q2 price matching = "investment" in consumer; promo intensity expected through Q4; mulligan → do it all over again. DKS comp gdnc held; margin gdnc reflects legacy-footwear promos + "conservatism toward fuel costs and health care costs."
- FL: Q1 FL US comp ~6% (McShane); Q2 "a disappointment" in launch/retro; EMEA "very difficult" w/ "a war going on in the Middle East"; "taking longer than we thought"; "creating a menu" of options, "no preconceived ideas".
- Fast Break: 250 remodels done, 350 by YE (McShane); "there's more editing to be done" beyond 30% SKU cut; more apparel/fixtures tested. Balance sheet "$1 billion on the balance sheet right now"; FL "pretty low capital-intensive."
- Ecosystem visibility: atmos (Tier 0, Tokyo) → FL street/HoS → DKS/traditional FL → GGG. "Five shoes" framework for HS athlete. Q2 GM +80bps attributed to DMN + GC; SG&A "running hard for some time now, it's not lost on us." HoS "fourth year"; "comping the comp" yrs 2-4; Vuori added via HoS. Coach by DICK'S AI app embedded in mobile app.
- Numbers: McShane "Foot Locker US was able to comp about 6% in the first quarter"; "you've only remodeled 250 stores to Fast Break, getting them to, I think, 350 by the end of the year." Stack price match "We can buy this shoe for $25 less expensive on such and such a site. We didn't want them to go someplace else to buy that product."; "We've got $1 billion on the balance sheet right now."; "we've owned it for a year and a week now."; Fast Break "we thought we could take 30% of the SKUs out... What we have determined is that there's more editing to be done"; "the 80/20 rule... When you take a look at the 20% of the sales, there's still too many SKUs in that 20%, it's too broad." Gupta "If you look at the 80 basis points of gross margin expansion that was driven here in second quarter, was driven by DICK'S Media Network and GameChanger."; synergy "$100 million to $125 million of synergy, and that's a collective company synergy." Hobart HoS "usually about 100,000 to 125,000 square foot"; "we're now in our fourth year"; "even in year two, year three, year four, we're comping the comp, and that's really important."; "we kept our comp guidance the same." Stack new brands "Vuori, we've got a number of stores, Gymshark, Free People Movement."
- Rapid-fire: 2H pricing Stack "I think it'll be similar to the second quarter... I wouldn't look at it half to half. I would look at it quarter-to-quarter"; 2027 margins Gupta no gdnc ("we haven't provided the guidance for 2027"); "clear focus and collective work that is being done across the organization on productivity."
- Quotes Stack: "I think, the idea that the athletic cycle is over, is overdone, okay?"; "Foot Locker on launch shoes was much more dependent on kind of the high-launch retro shoes than we are at DICK'S."; "this business has just broadened out which is really, we think, very good for us, the fact that there is more alternatives for us to go to."; "The margin pressure is real as these legacy silhouettes have slowed down, and then some of the brands started discounting, and the discounting got pretty aggressive. And we felt it was really important for us to stay with the market from a price standpoint."; "I use the word investment very purposely."; "We talk all the time that we make investments in our business not for a quarter or two, but for a lifetime."; "we had a mulligan to do it all over again, what we did in the second quarter, we'd do it all over again exactly the same way."; "a triple white Air Force One in patent leather, a triple white Air Force One in black, you can't keep those in stock today."; "What has surprised us and surprised the industry as a whole is the launch in retro product has been very difficult, and that's well chronicled. When we originally looked at it, giving our guidance for the year, we didn't anticipate that. And Q1 was pretty good, Q2 was pretty difficult."; "when we originally gave our guidance for the year of what we thought we were going to do from a Foot Locker standpoint, there was not a war going on in the Middle East, and that has had a really meaningful impact on Europe."; "the industry's got a really interesting problem that is short term, but they've got a capacity – an overcapacity issue in some of the legacy silhouettes, and there's an under-capacity issue on some of these new shoes."; "It's not that difficult. The consumer knows what's new and what's hot and what's not."; "New Balance is a bit more difficult at Foot Locker. It's rocking in DICK'S."; atmos "we own a company in Tokyo called atmos which is a Tier 0 retailer, very similar to Kith here in the US... So, we've got visibility to the entire ecosystem of athletic footwear and, to be honest, nobody else has got that kind of visibility that we have."; "believe me, this is not easy with Foot Locker... It's just taking longer than we anticipated and we thought it would be quicker. We thought some other brands would be able to be more supportive quicker than they have been, more legacy brands, and that hasn't happened."; "we are creating a menu that all of you would expect us to create based on the environment we're in today and what we're seeing. So, we've got no preconceived ideas of what has to be done. We're not going after this like, damn the torpedoes, we're going to make this thing work."; brand CEO anecdote "he said, in the past, we didn't really trust Foot Locker... now that you guys own it, he said, we are fully invested and supportive of Foot Locker."; "in retail and the way we've done things at DICK'S, we're never in the final stage."; "new brands that I would say we are not emerging anymore, but have emerged, such as On and Hoka have been great."; "You see other brands now coming up with that, Saucony, Salomon, ASICS is coming back, and what's going on with Timberland, with UGGs, Birkenstock."; "from a Nike standpoint, the Mind shoe has been terrific."; "Mall developers love House of Sport."; "We're going to continue and invest in this" (HoS); "The specialty channel has been – is a bit more challenged."
- Quotes Hobart: "Foot Locker often will experience a trend a little bit ahead of where DICK'S would experience a trend... we can taper our buy at DICK'S accordingly."; "is Foot Locker a distraction for DICK'S? I would say absolutely not. But on top of that, I think actually, it's helpful to the DICK'S Business."; "The consumer is, clearly across the country, is under pressure and we've heard the same in EMEA. But the DICK'S consumer is holding up very, very well."; "I think the Foot Locker consumer may be a little bit more under pressure."; "We are not doing for tech for tech's sake."; "We also reflected some conservatism toward fuel costs and health care costs."; "a consumer-facing app called Coach by DICK'S, which is now embedded into the DICK'S mobile app."
- Gupta: "that point that you made that it's been running hard for some time now, it's not lost on us and we are consciously focused on that."; "we are able to go and test these capabilities in House of Sport, how well they do. Then you are able to quickly move into the Field House concept."
- Attribution note: passage labeled "Kate McShane" in FactSet — "I think there's a concern out there that the contagion that is in Foot Locker will spread to DICK'S, and we don't see that... if you were to take a look at our comps in footwear, they're really quite good, you'd be pretty pleased... they really need five different shoes... between DICK'S and Foot Locker, we are the retailers best positioned to service that need" — is mgmt (first-person "our footwear business", "the DICK'S team"); context indicates Ed Stack.

### 4/5. Thematic quote bank & evolution tracker (only items not already above)
- Quote bank otherwise =same as §2-3.8 quotes (organized by theme: FL; footwear/Nike/vendors; HoS/Field House; core comp/consumer; margins/tariffs/pricing; GC/media/loyalty; capital allocation; competition; LT targets).
- Competition note: Academy Sports and Amazon not named by mgmt in any of these transcripts.
- Evolution tracker summary (by event 2024-09-05 → 2026-09-14):
  - HoS: 12 at end-2023, ~20 end-2024, 15 in 2025, 75-100 by 2027 → 19 YE24, 35 YE25 → "nothing is changing" (FL call) → 16 in 2025, 35 YE25, 15-16 in 2026, 75-100 by 2027 → 35 open, ~15/yr → 67-68 by end-2027 "meaningless difference"; 2026 capex just over 30 HoS incl 18 opening 2027 → 4th yr, comping the comp, "continue and invest".
  - FL turnaround: synergies $100-125M; Nike up at higher margin → "retail 101", clean up, more on Q4 call → 500-600bps erosion "we can reverse that trend", Q4 margin 1,000-1,500bps below, comps down MSD-HSD, BTS step change → not back in yr 1 but margin improvement, nothing structural → Q2 disappointment, longer than thought, menu, more Fast Break editing.
  - Footwear tone: "access will continue to be a really important part of our story" → "it's not just a two brand story", "tremendous long term potential" → engine that pulls train; ~50% combined → new doing well, older slower → Nike running "out of this world", "newness is a theme" → "We will be growing footwear", "We still have upside across" → athletic cycle over "overdone", legacy "really slowed", margin pressure real, not demand.
  - Core comp: Q2 FY24 +4.5% on +2% → Q3 FY24 +4.2% → Q1 FY25 prelim +4.5% → Q2 FY25 +5% on +0.5% → Q3 FY25 +5.7% → Q4 FY25 +3.1% on +6.6%, traffic down, 2026 plan 2-4% → comp gdnc kept.
  - GC: 6M athletes, $100M, 35-40% CAGR → $100M YE, 30-40%/yr, TAM $30-40B → $100M last yr→$150M, 40%+ CAGR → 9M users, TAM $40B, $100M incl media → ~$150M, ~40% CAGR 3 yrs → DMN+GC drove 80bps GM expansion Q2.
  - Vertical premium: 600-800bps → 600-800bps, $1.6B→$2B goal → 700-900bps → "even becoming better".
  - Balance sheet: sq ft +~2% → optimally levered, capex up to $800M, 100% FCF returned → $1.7B cash, cash+LTD+equity → 2026 new DC → capex $1.5B (analyst, unchallenged), dividends 12 straight yrs, buyback offsets dilution → $1B on balance sheet.
