### 2. Deal announcement 2025-05-15
#### 2.1 Investor presentation (Ex 99.3) — no slide text in EDGAR (≥12 pages); reconstructed from call slide references
- Disclaimer slide (Gilch). Slide 4: Stack "$140 billion" US TAM, >3,200 stores, "$300 billion global sports retail market". Slide 5: FL overview "a global footwear and athletic apparel retailer"; banners; ~2,400 stores across 26 countries end-2024; licensed ME/Europe/Asia; >400 refreshes; digital 18%; 2024 rev $8B (joint PR says "20 countries", call says "26 countries"). Slide 6: five benefits. Slide 7: US real estate "highly complementary"; intl expansion first time (Europe, ME, APAC); "$300 billion global total addressable market for sports retail." Slide 8: sneakerheads/lifestyle (FL) + performance (DKS). Slide 9: brand partner logos. Slide 10: DKS profitable growth history; FL digital modernization & refreshes; HoS and FL "reimagined concept store". Slide 11: efficiencies "primarily through procurement and direct sourcing." Terms slide (Gupta): $24/sh; cash or 0.1168; no min/max; equity ~$2.4B; EV $2.5B; $100-125M cost synergies medium term; EPS-accretive first full FY post-close ex one-time; committed to IG ratings; unanimous boards; close 2H25.
- Slide cited by Yih: "the 25% that you have of merchandise purchases and the 59% of Foot Locker's merchandise purchases" (single-vendor concentration DKS ~25%, FL 59%; vendor not named in transcript). Other slide numbers not in document.
#### 2.2 Joint PR (Ex 99.2) exact
- Headline "DICK'S Sporting Goods to Acquire Foot Locker to Create a Global Leader in the Sports Retail Industry". "This transaction implies an equity value of approximately $2.4 billion and an enterprise value of approximately $2.5 billion."
- FL: "approximately 2,400 retail stores across 20 countries in North America, Europe, Asia, Australia and New Zealand, and a licensed store presence in Europe, the Middle East and Asia. In 2024, Foot Locker achieved net worldwide sales of $8 billion. DICK'S expects to operate Foot Locker as a standalone business unit within its portfolio and maintain the Foot Locker brands."
- Consideration "(i) $24.00 in cash or (ii) 0.1168 shares ... not subject to a minimum or maximum".
- "Based on the closing price of Foot Locker common stock on 5/14/2025, the $24.00 per-share consideration represents a premium of approximately 66% to Foot Locker's 60-trading day volume weighted average price. The total consideration represents an acquisition multiple of approximately 6.1x fiscal 2024 adjusted EBITDA."
- "DICK'S intends to finance the acquisition through a combination of cash-on-hand and new debt."
- "accretive to EPS in the first full fiscal year post-close (excludes transaction and other one-time costs to achieve synergies) and to deliver between $100 to $125 million in cost synergies in the medium-term achieved through procurement and direct sourcing efficiencies." Close "expected to close in the second half of 2025", subject to FL vote + regulatory.
- Advisors: Goldman Sachs (DKS FA; "provided fully committed bridge financing"); Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz (DKS legal); Evercore (FL FA); Skadden, Arps, Slate, Meagher & Flom (FL legal).
- 5 rationale headings verbatim: "Create a global platform within the growing sports retail industry."; "Serve a broader set of consumers across differentiated concepts."; "Strengthen relationships with brand partners through global reach."; "Invest in future growth through an industry-leading omnichannel experience."; "Unlock operational efficiencies that create shareholder value."
- Quotes: Stack ("We have long admired the cultural significance and brand equity that Foot Locker and its dedicated Stripers have built…"); Hobart ("…we'll create a new global platform…"); Mary Dillon, FL CEO ("…offers the choice of significant and immediate cash value or the opportunity to invest in the combined company…"). DKS boilerplate: "more than 850 DICK'S Sporting Goods, Golf Galaxy, Public Lands and Going Going Gone! stores".
#### 2.3 Prelim Q1 FY2025 PR (Ex 99.1): qtr ended 2025-05-03 comps +4.5%; diluted EPS $3.24; non-GAAP $3.37 (adds back $0.13 "Investment losses … non-cash losses from non-operating investment in equity securities"). Q1 call 2025-05-28 8:00 ET.
#### 2.4 Merger Agreement (Item 1.01)
- Parties DKS; RJS Sub LLC (NY LLC, wholly owned); Foot Locker, Inc. (NY corp). Merger Sub merges into FL; FL survives as wholly owned sub.
- Election period "not less than twenty business days ending on the date that is five business days prior to DICK'S good faith estimate of the Closing date."
- Equity awards: employee RSUs/PSUs → DKS time-based RSUs on stock consideration (perf goals deemed achieved per award agmt); non-employee director RSUs/deferred units vest, cash out at cash consideration; options cashed out for spread over $24.00 (OTM cancelled for no consideration).
- Conditions: 2/3 of outstanding FL common; HSR + "the antitrust laws of certain other jurisdictions"; no Restraint; S-4 effective; reps/covenants; no FL MAE.
- Financing: commitment letter 2025-05-15 w/ Goldman Sachs Bank USA "up to $2.4 billion of senior bridge term loans" for cash consideration, repay certain FL debt, fees.
- Outside date 2026-05-15 "subject to two automatic extensions of three months each if on such date all of the closing conditions except those relating to regulatory approvals have been satisfied or waived"; 30-day cure.
- Fees: FL pays $59,500,000 on change of recommendation/Superior Proposal; DKS pays FL $95,500,000 "if the Merger Agreement is terminated in connection with a failure to obtain the requisite antitrust approvals." Ex 2.1 schedules omitted.
#### 2.5 Investor call 2025-05-15 =same as SRC Conference_and_Event §2 (FL call); differences/additions here:
- Analyst premise "close to 3 times leverage" (Yih) neither confirmed nor denied. Gupta: "committed to maintaining our investment grade ratings."
- Stack distribution: brands "they really want to be in a specialty type environment."; Ohmes: "I think Foot Locker will get more high heat product … the Nike business will probably go up. We expect that Nike business will grow up at a higher margin rate".
- Hobart closures: "that team has obviously done an assessment of all of their stores. They've indicated there are some that are potentially being closed"; banners "our plan right now is to run them as separate entities".
- Stack to Lasser: "we're not going to provide any information right now … when we close we'll provide what we think this — what the camp [sic] is going to be..."; Hobart market share "around 80%" [as transcribed; likely 8%].
#### 2.6 Q1 FY25 call excerpts 2025-05-28 (FL-related)
- Stack: DKS "positions us to participate in a $300 billion global sports retail market, and expands our reach to over 3,200 stores worldwide"; "We understand that there's really a group of people out there, shareholders that would really have preferred we just continue to do what we're doing … We don't think that's right long term"; "we don't make investments or decisions for a quarter or two. We make these decisions and investments for a lifetime."
- Hobart (to Gutman) Nike: "we've heard about some distribution changes … Nike … are very good at segmenting their products … We expect minimal overlap with some of the new distribution … The running construct, some of the lifestyle apparel, women's basketball."
- Gupta FTC: "we anticipate that would be somewhere in the second half of this year."
- Divestiture threshold $100M (analyst cited); Stack: "if we have to divest a lot, then it kind of makes it not consistent with what our strategy and the tactics are … that's why we've got that $100 million number there."
- Hobart: "about 30% of our stores are in malls … We'll have access, even within the US, to urban locations that we don't have access to before with the large format stores." Revenue synergies: "we'll share much more detailed points when the transaction is closed."
- Buybacks "Nearly 300 million in the first quarter" (analyst); Gupta "there are certain restrictions as we are in the phase of the S4 filing." Gilch: Q2 FY25 results tentatively 2025-09-03. Integration: "we are going to put a small group of people working for Ed to work with the Foot Locker team … The gross margin improvement that we know is available".
- May 28 PR financing: "through a combination of cash-on-hand, revolving borrowings and other new debt, to the degree Foot Locker shareholders do not elect to receive their consideration entirely in shares of the Company's common stock."

### 3. Employee communications (no vendor/customer FAQs exist)
- Teammate letter: "After closing, Foot Locker will continue to operate as a separate entity, reporting directly into Ed. Until the deal is final, Foot Locker is an independent company and will continue to operate as such … There are no expected changes to our operations, our team, or our strategic priorities."
- DKS video (Stack): "we expect Foot Locker will continue to operate as an independent brand … a core tenant of this deal is that it CANNOT distract our team from delivering our growth plan." Hobart 3 benefits: global platform; broader consumers across differentiated banners; strengthen global brand partner relationships.
- FL video (Stack): "We expect Foot Locker to continue to be operated as a separate business … with the coolest shoes sold by coolest stripers to the coolest kids … Together we will return Foot Locker to its rightful place in the industry." Hobart: DKS values "Dedication, Optimism, Integrity, and Authenticity"; "joined this company 14 years ago." Employee comms reference "global brand partners" without naming Nike. HQ: not in documents.
- Closing PR 2025-09-08: "DICK'S will continue to operate Foot Locker's portfolio of brands … under an experienced new leadership team. DICK'S Executive Chairman, Ed Stack, will lead the global Foot Locker businesses, in partnership with two new presidents, one for North America and one for International … Ann Freeman, a longtime former Nike executive, has been appointed President of Foot Locker North America. DICK'S will appoint a President of Foot Locker International to lead other regions." NA team: Tony Aversa (SVP & GM FL & Kids FL NA; 30-yr FL veteran, most recently GM Champs); Denise Karkos (SVP & GM Champs; from DKS SVP Chief eCommerce Officer); George Jenkins (SVP Store Ops & Customer Experience; 30 yrs FL); Peter Scaturro (SVP CFO FL NA; ex SVP Strategic Planning & Growth FL); Steve Miller (SVP COO FL NA; former DKS senior exec Hardlines Merch, Strategy/eCom/Analytics); Brett O'Brien (SVP CMO; from PepsiCo, Chief Sports Officer); Michael Keinath (SVP Chief People Officer; from DKS). Mary Dillon post-close role not in document.
- Closing PR: "accelerate business momentum and targeted turnaround strategies"; synergies $100-125M "primarily through procurement and direct sourcing efficiencies"; "accretive to EPS in Fiscal Year 2026 (excluding transaction and other one-time costs to achieve synergies)." Footprint: "more than 3,200 stores plus e-commerce and digital businesses across 20 countries in North America, Europe, Asia, and Australia, plus a licensed store presence in Europe, the Middle East and Asia."

### 4. Exchange offer & financing
#### 4.1 FL 4.000% Sr Notes due 2029 exchange/consent
| Term | Detail |
|---|---|
| Target | FL 4.000% due 10/01/2029; $400,000,000; CUSIP/ISIN 144A 344849AA2/US344849AA21; Reg S U3449AAA5/USU3449AAA52 |
| Offered | up to $400M new DKS 4.000% due 2029 (same dates/maturity/rate) + cash consent; eligible holders only (QIBs/non-US persons, not EEA/UK retail) |
| Exchange consideration | $970 DKS notes per $1,000 + $30 early premium = $1,000 |
| Consent payment | $2.50 × (principal outstanding at early date ÷ principal tendered by early date): $2.50 (100%) to ~$5.00 (simple majority); actual ≈$2.71/$1,000 |
| DKS notes redemption | "replace the fixed redemption schedule currently included in the Foot Locker Notes with a customary investment grade redemption schedule, including a 3-month par call date and make-whole mechanism" (S-4: make-whole T+25bp; Par Call 2029-07-01) |
| Amendments | "eliminate substantially all of the restrictive covenants, certain affirmative covenants and certain events of default"; supplemental indenture executed 2025-06-20; operative only on closing/settlement |
| Conditions | majority tender (waivable); acquisition closing (not waivable); acquisition not conditioned on exchange |
| Dates | launch 2025-06-06; early participation/consent revocation 5pm NYC 2025-06-20; orig expiration 2025-08-01; extended (08-04) to 2025-08-29; settlement 2025-09-11 |
| Results | 06-20 $369,381,000 (92.35%); 08-01 $379,435,000 (94.86%); at settlement DKS issued $381.9M ($381,932,000 per S-4) DKS 4.000% due 2029-10-01; $18.1M remained FL Notes |
| Agents | Global Bondholder Services Corp (exchange/info); dealer manager not named |
| Registration rights | agmt "with the dealer manager": registered exchange consummated by 366th day after 2025-09-11 (by 2026-09-12) else additional interest |
#### 4.2 $2.4B bridge & replacement
- 2025-05-15 GS Bank commitment up to $2.4B. Sept 5 PF: commitment "supplemented by the joinder on May 30, 2025 of certain other financial institutions"; "a senior unsecured 364-day bridge term loan credit facility in an aggregate principal amount of up to $2.4 billion"; "In June 2025, the aggregate Bridge Facility commitments were reduced to $1.75 billion, following the receipt of requisite consents … in connection with Company's $400.0 million Exchange Offer … as well as the Company's entry into a new revolving credit facility."
- June 6/23 PFs assumed "unsecured senior notes for an amount of $1.7 billion with a fixed interest rate of 6.4% per annum" (Scen A): proceeds $1,732,000K − $12,793K costs = $1,719,207K; annual int $110,365K + $576K amortization.
- Outcome: ~92.6% elected stock → cash consideration only ~$0.2B ($222,447K); Sept 5 PF has no Financing Adjustments; Sept 21, 2026 PF "approximately $0.2 billion of cash consideration and approximately 9.6 million shares." Aug 1, 2026 cap table: no acquisition term debt, revolver undrawn. Whether bridge ever drawn not stated (PF shows no draw).
#### 4.3 2025-06-06 revolver
- $2.0B unsecured; Wells Fargo Bank N.A. admin agent; ≤$75M LCs; matures 2030-06-06; ABR+0.125% or adj SOFR+1.125% initially "subject to adjustment based on the Company's public debt rating"; no amortization; prepay w/o premium (SOFR breakage only). Covenants: liens, fundamental changes, non-guarantor sub debt; "a maximum lease-adjusted leverage ratio covenant" (2026 424B2: "a maximum consolidated total leverage ratio"). Ex 10.1. Replaced A&R Credit Agmt 2022-01-14 (commitments terminated, repaid 2025-06-06).
#### 4.4 PF leverage/capitalization
- PF leverage ratios (debt/EBITDA) not in documents.
- LTD & finance leases: June 6 PF (2025-02-01): DKS $1,484,217K; FL $441,000K; Scen A PF $3,626,275K (incl $1,719,207K new notes); B $1,907,068K. June 23 (2025-05-03): DKS $1,484,462K; FL $440,000K; A $3,625,339K; B $1,906,132K. Sept 5 (2025-08-02): DKS $1,484,707K; FL $440,000K; PF $1,903,808K (FL notes FV adj $(22,372)K; deferred financing reversal +$1,473K).

#### 6.2 Final 424B2 economics (supplement 2026-09-22 to prospectus 2026-09-21)
- Price to public $399,412,000 (2036)/$599,772,000 (2056); UW discounts 0.650% ($2,600,000)/0.875% ($5,250,000); proceeds before expenses $396,812,000/$594,522,000; offering exp est $2.9M; net "approximately $988 million". Dealer concessions ≤0.400%/0.500%; reallowance ≤0.250%/0.350%.
- UW allocations (2036/2056): BofA $120M/$180M; Wells Fargo $92M/$138M; PNC $64M/$96M; U.S. Bancorp $40M/$60M; Goldman $20M/$30M; TD $20M/$30M; Truist $20M/$30M; Capital One $16M/$24M; Citizens JMP $4M/$6M; HSBC $4M/$6M.
- "affiliates of some of the underwriters are lenders, and in some cases agents or managers for the lenders, under the Revolving Credit Facility"; "U.S. Bancorp Investments, Inc., one of the underwriters, is an affiliate of the Trustee." Trustee U.S. Bank Trust Company, N.A.; base indenture 2022-01-14 + supplemental dated 2026-09-25; NY law; no listing.
- Use of proceeds (p. S-15): "We estimate the net proceeds to us from the sale of the notes will be approximately $988 million, after deducting underwriting discounts and estimated offering expenses payable by us. We expect to use the net proceeds from the offering of the notes for general corporate purposes, which may include but are not limited to financing our operations, repayment of debt, repurchases of shares of our common stock and future business acquisitions."
#### 6.4 Covenants/CoC/EoD
- Unsecured unsubordinated; pari passu w/ revolver & "Existing Senior Notes" ("the Company's 4.00% senior notes due 2029, 3.15% senior notes due 2032 and 4.10% senior notes due 2052"); structurally subordinated to subs.
- "As of August 1, 2026, pro forma for the notes offered hereby, the Company would have had approximately $13.5 billion of unsecured, unsubordinated indebtedness outstanding (including approximately $6.1 billion of operating leases) and no secured indebtedness outstanding." Subs: "approximately $3.9 billion of outstanding liabilities, including trade payables and deferred revenue."
- Liens: no debt secured by Voting Stock of Significant Subsidiary w/o equal & ratable, exceptions incl basket greater of 15% CNTA and $1,950,000,000; Significant Sub = >10% consolidated total assets, excl non-US subs; CNTA PF for acquisitions/dispositions >$500,000,000. Mergers/sale of substantially all assets limitation. No financial-ratio maintenance, sale/leaseback, restricted-payments limits; indenture "will not limit the amount of indebtedness."
- CoC Triggering Event = CoC (>50% voting power or sale of substantially all assets) + Below IG Rating Event (both agencies below Baa3/BBB- within 60-day Relevant Period). "Permitted Person" = Edward W. Stack + Affiliated Parties (and group in which they hold ≥50% of group's voting stock). 101% put; waiver by majority; 90% clean-up redemption at 101%.
- EoD: 30-day interest default; principal default; 90-day covenant default after notice from Trustee or ≥25% holders; bankruptcy; acceleration by Trustee or >25%. Customary satisfaction/discharge/defeasance. RF: revolver "requires the Company to comply with a maximum consolidated total leverage ratio"; credit agmt 2025-06-06.

### 3. Results per quarter
#### Q3 FY2024 (2024-11-26)
- Comp +4.2% (calendar-shift adj); ticket +4.8%, txns −0.6%; 3rd straight >4%. Net sales +0.5% to $3.06B; calendar shift (53rd wk LY) moved key BTS wk out of Q3: ~−$105M sales, −$0.35 EPS.
- GP $1.09B, 35.77%, +67bps vs non-GAAP LY; merch margin +84bps ("favorable sales mix and the quality of our assortment") (Q&A: "83 basis points"), partly offset by occupancy deleverage (calendar).
- SG&A non-GAAP +7.2% to $787.1M, delev 162bps (~65bps calendar; rest marketing, tech, talent, higher incentive comp). Pre-opening $16.8M, −$3.6M (timing). EBT $297.1M, 9.7% vs $321.1M/10.6% non-GAAP LY (~95bps calendar). EPS $2.75 vs $2.85 non-GAAP LY (−$0.35 calendar).
- YTD 39 wks: sales +4.8% to $9.55B; comp +4.7% (ticket +3.7%, txns +1%); EBT $1.12B/11.75% vs $975.3M/10.71%; EPS $10.43 vs $9.08 (+15%). Calendar: 9-month benefit +$35M/+$0.10 YTD; Q4 headwind −$30M/−$0.10; 53rd wk FY23 $170M sales/$0.19 EPS; FY24 midpoint comp ~4% vs FY23 2.6%.
- Cash ~$1.5B; $1.6B facility undrawn; inventory +13% (deliberate key-item investment; "clearance inventory is down meaningfully year-over-year"). Net capex ~$185M; dividends $90M; ~35,000 shs repurchased for $6.7M at $194.22; YTD buybacks $170.3M.
- HoS 19 (3 in Q3 + 2 in Nov); FH 26 (5 in Q3 + 4 in Nov); 12 HoS end-2023, ~20 end-2024; ~2% sq ft growth 2024; 90% stores premium full-service footwear decks; Fort Worth TX DC broke ground (open early 2026).
- GC >5.5M unique active users Q3 (+21%); ~2M avg DAU; streamed games +50%; ~$100M 2024 rev; 30-40% CAGR. 1.5M more athletes entered ecosystem in qtr; vertical brands 600-800bps higher margin than national.
- Slide 11 (per Kernan): $35M HoS/$14M FH Y1 omni sales; 20% four-wall omni EBITDA.
#### Q1 FY2025 (2025-05-28)
- Comp +4.5% (ticket +3.7%, txns +0.8%); 2-yr 9.8%, 3-yr 13.4%; 5th straight >4%. Sales +5.2% to $3.17B. GP $1.17B, 36.7%, +41bps (merch margin). SG&A non-GAAP +7% to $791.2M, delev 42bps (digital, in-store, marketing; partly offset lower incentive comp). Pre-opening $13.4M, −$7.7M.
- Non-GAAP OI $360.4M, 11.35% vs $334.5M/11.08%. Other income $13.3M (−$8.3M). EBT $361.6M, 11.39% vs $342.4M/11.34%. Tax ~24% vs 19.6% (~440bps; LY ~$0.19 equity-award benefit). EPS non-GAAP $3.37 (+2.1% vs $3.30); GAAP $3.24 (non-cash losses on FL stock).
- Cash ~$1B; no borrowings on $1.6B facility; inventory +12%. Net capex $242M; dividends $100M; repurchased 1.4M shs for $298.7M at $218.65.
- 2 HoS in Q1 + 1 in May; 4 FH in Q1 + 2 more; ~16 HoS & ~16 FH 2025; YE ~35 HoS, "just over 40" FH; ~20 HoS planned 2026; GGPC 27 → 35 in 2025; ~30% of DSG stores in malls.
- GC >6.5M unique active users; ~2.2M DAU (+~28%); >$100M 2024 rev → $150M; 40-50% top-line growth; Unrivaled Sports investment (600,000 youth athletes, 2M families; ~$40B youth-sports TAM).
- >20M new athletes over 3 yrs; ~$140B US TAM, ~8% share; no tariff impact Q1.
#### Q2 FY2025 (2025-08-28)
- Comp +5% (ticket +4.1%, txns +0.9%) on +4.5% LY, +2% 2023; 2-yr 9.5%, 3-yr 11.5%; 6th straight >4%. Sales +5% to $3.65B.
- GP $1.35B, 37.06%, +33bps (merch +18bps + occupancy leverage); "minimal impact from tariffs in Q2." SG&A non-GAAP +9.9% to $864M, delev 105bps. Pre-opening $12.3M, +$3.4M.
- Non-GAAP OI $475M, 13.02% vs $480.5M/13.83%. EBT $472.6M, 12.96% vs $482.3M/13.89%. EPS non-GAAP $4.38 vs $4.37; GAAP $4.71 (non-cash FL stock gains; merger/integration/financing costs).
- Cash ~$1.2B; no borrowings on new $2B unsecured facility; inventory +7.1%. Net capex $213M; dividends $96M; no repurchases (S-4 restrictions).
- 1 HoS Q2, 13 planned Q3 (record); 4 FH Q2, 6 Q3; YE ~35 HoS/~42 FH. GC 7.4M unique active users; 5.5M MAU (+16%); ~50% rev growth off >$100M.
- ~1% of comp from txns, rest basket; vertical 700-900bps higher margin; #1 or #2 vendor in accessories, athletic apparel, fitness, golf, team sports; Q4 2024 comp 6.4%; 2H LY comps ~50bps stronger than 1H. FL shs (per Horvers) ~11M.
#### Q3 FY2025 (2025-11-25; first combined, 8 wks FL)

- FL PF comp (full Q3) −4.7%; NA −2.6%; Intl −10.2% ("primarily driven by softness in Europe").

- Non-GAAP OI consol $242.2M, 5.81% vs $289.5M/9.47%; DKS Biz $288.6M, 8.92%; FL op loss $46.3M (PF full-Q3 FL OI ~$6.8M). Other income $12.7M (−$7.8M). EBT $239.9M, 5.76% vs $297.1M/9.7%. Tax $59.4M, 24.7% (DKS low-20%s; FL EMEA full valuation allowance).
- EPS consol non-GAAP $2.07; DKS Biz $2.78 (81.2M shs) vs $2.75; FL ops −$0.52, share count −$0.19 (5.9M shs prorated); GAAP $0.86 incl $141.9M pretax acq costs.
- Cash ~$821M; no borrowings on $2B; inventory +51% consol, DKS +2%, FL PF ~+5%. Net capex $218M ($201M DKS, $17M FL); dividends $109M.

- FY25 consol: sales +28.1% to $17.22B ($3.11B FL, ~20 wks); non-GAAP OI $1.52B, 8.81% vs $1.5B/11.14%; consol non-GAAP EPS $13.20 on 85.1M shs.

- Consol sales +53.2% to $5.59B ($1.74B FL). DKS Biz total sales +5.6%; comp +4.9% (ticket +3.6%, txns +1.3%); 2-yr 9.9%, 3-yr 14.4%; "outpaced the broader industry by nearly 200 basis points." FL PF comp −3.6% (NA and Intl both down).

Tariff refunds ~$59M ($57M DKS/$2M FL); $21M in non-GAAP ($19M DKS merch margin, $2M FL), $38M excluded as one-time.

- Cash ~$914M; no borrowings on $2B; inventory $5.57B; DKS inventory +6% ("in line with our total sales growth"). Net capex $325M; dividends $111M; no repurchases cited.

- FL: charges to date $516M; Fast Break >250, >300-350 by YE; first major brand campaign ("Colors") in more than a decade; Striper payroll increased. ScoreCard ~30M active athletes; ScoreCard+ paid tier $99/yr launched (July 2026 per slides).

### 4. Per-call detail
#### 4.1 Q3 FY2024 call 2024-11-26 (Hobart, Gupta)
- Prepared: Hobart "Our Q3 comps increased 4.2%, driven by our four strategic pillars of omnichannel athlete experience, differentiated product assortment, deep engagement with the DICK's brand, and our knowledgeable and passionate teammates who are integral to our success."; "We now expect comp sales growth for the full year to be in the range of 3.6% to 4.2%, and EPS to be in the range of $13.65 to $13.95."; "In Q3, we opened three House of Sport locations, followed by two more earlier this month, bringing us to 19 now open ahead of the holiday season. In 2025, we expect to open approximately 15 House of Sport locations and we remain on track to have 75 to 100 locations opened by 2027." / "In Q3, we opened five Field House locations, followed by another four earlier this month, bringing us to 26 now open. In 2025, we expect to open approximately 20 Field House locations."; "This quarter, we broke ground on the new distribution center we've been planning in Fort Worth, Texas, which is expected to open in early 2026."; GC "Over 5.5 million unique users were active on GameChanger in the third quarter, a 21% increase from last year, and we had approximately 2 million average daily active users in the GameChanger app during the quarter."
- Gupta: "Our quarter-end inventory levels increased 13% compared to Q3 of last year... we have made a conscious decision to lean into key items and categories which we expect to drive our growth in Q4 as well as into early 2025... In fact, our clearance inventory is down meaningfully year-over-year."; "We continue to expect EBT margins to be at 11.2% of sales at the midpoint. In total, we now anticipate earnings per diluted share to be in the range of $13.65 to $13.95 compared to our prior expectation of $13.55 to $13.90. Our earnings guidance is based on approximately 83 million average diluted shares outstanding and an effective tax rate of 23%. We continue to expect net capital expenditures of approximately $800 million for the year."; "In total, Q4 compares versus last year will be unfavorably impacted by approximately $200 million in sales and approximately $0.29 in earnings per diluted share."; "We also repurchased approximately 35,000 shares of our stock for $6.7 million at an average price of $194.22. Thus far this year, we have repurchased a total of $170.3 million of our stock. For the full year, we continue to expect share repurchases of approximately $300 million."
- Q Yih (Barclays) why DKS wins in promo environment: A Hobart "we have had three quarters in a row now of over a 4% comp"; "We saw 1.5 million more athletes enter our ecosystem over the past quarter. And we saw growth across all income demographics."; women's basketball, expanded NCAA playoffs, 2026 World Cup, 2028 LA Olympics; "The country is really having what we're calling a smart moment."
- Q Yih tariffs (25% Canada/Mexico, 10% China): A Gupta "Vertical brands perspective, we have a very small or actually very, very negligible amount of exposure because we have diversified our supply chain"; 2018/2019 playbook.
- Q Gutman (MS) inventory/weather: A Hobart "we don't peanut butter spread the inventory across the categories. We're very surgical and strategic about it"; "broken on size and color for some of our key items, like Nike fleece"; earlier spring transition in warm markets. Gupta "The inventory is right in line with our expectation."
- Q Gutman SG&A vs margin: A Gupta "at the midpoint of our guidance, our comp will be approximately 4%, and which is on top of a 2.6% comp growth last year... the merch (sic) [EBT] margin at the midpoint will expand on a 40 basis points on a year-over-year basis as well."; "look to us to continue to drive strong top-line and strong profitability improvement with little bit of an interplay between the margin and SG&A."; "we are leveraging that to make strategic investments in SG&A to position ourselves better even for 2025 and beyond."
- Q Nagel (Oppenheimer) HoS rollout/comp impact: A Hobart "landlords and mall operators are noticing that when we put House of Sport into a mall, we get incredible, there's incredible traffic increases to the mall." Gupta "we finished last year with 12 House of Sport location and we expect to finish this year with approximately 20 House of Sport location... another 15 locations in 2025, with the long-term expectations of 75 to 100 House of Sport locations by 2027... the vast majority of these House of Sport openings will be relocation or remodels of the existing locations, and they will continue to remain in our comp."
- Q Nagel Q4 gdnc/shorter holiday: A Hobart "We did want to be appropriately cautious given the two factors, one being just the uncertain macroeconomic environment and then also the fewer holiday days." Gupta "we have raised our top line expectations for Q4."
- Q McShane (GS) overlap/differentiation: A Hobart "We don't release the exact amount that our products overlap." Gupta vertical brands CALIA, VRST, DSG drive differentiation & basket. Q ticket drivers: A Gupta "it's again, mix, the differentiated product, and the pricing opportunity that we see."; "four core categories where we are focused on driving our business and those are athletic apparel, athletic footwear, team sports and golf."
- Q Ohmes (BofA) GC economics/HoS brands: A Hobart "we are now forecasting for this year $100 million in GameChanger revenue, and it's a highly profitable software-as-a-subscription model." Gupta "it's growing at 30% to 40% CAGR each year and that has been the CAGR of this business for the last several years... we believe that the youth's tech landscape or youth sports tech landscape is multi-billion dollars"; Hobart cites GOAT (lacrosse), Free People Movement via Co Lab spaces.
- Q Kernan (TD Cowen) HoS yr-2/3 productivity (slide $35M HoS/$14M FH Y1 omni sales, 20% four-wall EBITDA): A Gupta "On the trajectory that we are seeing in year two, where these stores comp positively in the second year as well, because the excitement continues to build." Q footwear decks/HOKA/On: A Gupta "we call it footwear is the engine that drives the train"; "90% of our stores today have premium full service footwear deck. And we see opportunities to continue to expand ON, HOKA further deeper into our chain."
- Q Wasserman (JPM) Q4 merch margin/shrink: A Gupta "our expectation is that our merch margin will increase again in fourth quarter... our expectation for shrink for Q4 is to be relatively flat on a year-over-year basis." Q election/weather: A Hobart "It was warmer than we might have liked in the last part of the quarter, but it didn't have a material impact on our comps."
- Q Lasser (UBS) 2025 merch margin: A Gupta 3 plays — differentiated assortment/full-price, pricing-model surgical promos, vertical: "Vertical brands carry 600 basis points to 800 basis points of higher margins compared to the national brands." "The biggest unknown right now... is around tariffs." Q inventory +~$0.5B/Q4 margin math: A Hobart "things that we don't think are going to become toxic." Gupta Q4 held back by ~$200M unfavorable sales compare, higher Q4 pre-opening, "some level of caution."
- Q Crago/Lejuez (Citi) SG&A leverage point: A Gupta "I don't know if I want to give a specific number associated with that"; 2025 gdnc next qtr. 2025 openings: "the 15 House of Sport openings are just House of Sport openings. On top of that, we expect to open an additional 20 Field House locations... we are seeing only a 2% increase in square footage on a year-over-year basis."
- Q Kleber (Baird) softer exit/txns: A Hobart "I just don't want you to take away that, that we had a dip in the tail end of the quarter." Gupta "the Golf business actually exceeded our expectations in the back half." HoS elements in FH: Hobart FH "looks just like a House of Sport, a smaller version of a House of Sport."
- Tone: confident, 2nd raise FY24; pushback inventory +13% and SG&A deleverage; tariffs first appear as question; no FL.
#### 4.2 Q1 FY2025 call 2025-05-28 (Stack, Hobart, Gupta)
- Stack (first appearance): "This combination positions us to participate in a $300 billion global sports retail market, and expands our reach to over 3,200 stores worldwide."; "we expect the transaction to be accretive to DICK'S EPS in the first full fiscal year post close, and we see a clear path to unlocking meaningful cost synergies over the medium term."
- Hobart: "This is the fifth straight quarter where our team has delivered over 4% comp growth."; "over the past three years we've acquired over 20 million new athletes."; "we are reaffirming the guidance we provided for 2025, which includes the expected impact from all tariffs currently in effect. We continue to expect our comp sales to be in the range of 1% to 3%, which at the midpoint represents nearly a 10% three-year comp stack. We continue to expect our EPS to be in the range of $13.80 to $14.40."; 3 growth areas "repositioning our real estate and store portfolio, driving continued strong growth in key categories, and accelerating our e-commerce business."; GC "we had over 6.5 million unique active users during the first quarter, with an average of approximately 2.2 million daily active users, a nearly 28% year-over-year increase."; "more athletes purchased from us, they purchased more frequently, and they spent more each trip."
- Gupta: tax "our Q1 tax rate grew from 19.6% last year to approximately 24% this year. This approximate 440 basis points increase reflects the higher tax deduction from greater number of employee equity awards being exercised in the prior year, which favorably impacted Q1 2024 earnings by approximately $0.19."; outlook "We continue to expect comp sales growth in the range of 1% to 3%, with comps closer to the high end of our guidance through the third quarter."; "we also continue to expect gross margins to improve by approximately 75 basis points at the midpoint."; "We continue to expect operating margin to be approximately 11.1% at the midpoint."; "approximately 81 million average diluted shares outstanding compared to the prior expectation of 82 million and an effective tax rate of approximately 24%. We continue to expect net capital expenditures of approximately $1 billion for the year."; "we continue to expect greater SG&A expense deleverage in the first half, with moderation in the second half as we lap the higher investment levels from the second half of the last year."
- Q Nagel market missing on FL: A Stack "We understand that there's really a group of people out there, shareholders that would really have preferred we just continued to do what we're doing."; "We've kind of talked that we'll capture $100 million to $125 million of synergies through the medium term, and we've been very clear that we believe this will be accretive to our earnings in the first full fiscal year following the close."; "like it or not, we don't make investments or decisions for a quarter or two. We make these decisions and investments for a lifetime, and we do know that it's up to us to prove to the Street and to everybody that this was the right decision to make."; "It really gives us a unique opportunity to strengthen our brand relationships through a global presence, gives us the ability to service a portion of the market that we just we can't service today with our DICK'S Sporting Goods stores."; "This gives us an opportunity to compete for that market share and not just abdicate it to other retailers around the globe."
- Q Nagel tariffs: A Hobart "this quarter we actually saw no trade down from best to better to better to good. We saw growth across all income demographics, and we saw growth in ticket and transaction."; "We have an incredibly dynamic pricing ability."
- Q Gutman why only 1-3%: A Hobart "We do have higher comps that we're lapping in the back half of the year, and so that's a factor." Q Nike: A Hobart "Nike is a very important strategic partner for us... one thing that you can say about Nike time in and time out is that they are very good at segmenting their products, and we have no reason to expect that that won't be the same. So we expect segmentation of the market. We expect minimal overlap with some of the new distribution."; "The running construct, some of the lifestyle apparel, women's basketball, there's a lot of great stuff going on. So we feel terrific about the Nike partnership."
- Q Yih price increases: A Hobart "We are constantly assessing our pricing down to the item level, SKU level"; "our margin, we just guided at the midpoint, up 75 basis points."; "we have now factored in all of the known tariffs into our guidance. We are able to affirm our guidance going forward, both top line and bottom line and 75 basis points of gross margin improvement." Q inventory/tariff timing: A Gupta "Our inventory growth was 12%... we expect that the inventory growth will moderate, even with some of the tariff headwinds."
- Q Ohmes share gains/FTC: A Hobart "We have been operating in a $140 billion TAM in the US... we only have an 8% market share, despite all of the growth that we've had." Gupta FTC "somewhere in the second half of this year." Amazon/Nike: Hobart "We expect minimal overlap."
- Q Baker Unrivaled: A Hobart GC "That business, over $100 million last year, growing to $150 million, highly profitable, software-as-a-subscription business"; Unrivaled "hosting 600,000 youth athletes, 2 million families"; "the DICK'S Media Network is that there is an opportunity for in-game advertising. Again, it's a live sport platform." Gupta "this overall $40 billion TAM."
- Q McShane category cadence: A Hobart growth footwear, apparel, team sports; February "a little cold and wet."
- Q Horvers tariffed inventory: A Gupta "we have no impact from tariffs in Q1." $100M divestiture: Stack "if we have to divest a lot, then it kind of makes it not, like, consistent with what our strategy and the tactics are."
- Q Feldman (Telsey) Golf Galaxy: A Hobart "we've got 27 GGPC, Golf Galaxy Performance Centers that are going to 35 this year"; "we are our own number one vendor partner in golf with vertical brands."; GC crossover "the people who do crossover... are some of our absolute best shoppers." Gupta Bat Lab — 20 players tested 12 BBCOR bats.
- Q Lasser temporary factors/allocations: A Hobart "I don't at all think that our growth has been driven by unique and temporary factors."; "we're 90% now" premium footwear decks; "we will now be partnering – these are all global brands, so we'll be partnering with them on even longer-term global product innovation."
- Q Kernan biggest FL opportunity: A Hobart "we are going to put a small group of people working for Ed to work with the Foot Locker team to really unlock all of that, the gross margin improvement that we know is available." Gupta "will be about 35 House of Sport locations by the end of this year, just over 40 Field House locations... we anticipate opening about 20 House of Sport locations in 2026."
- Q Lejuez FL overlap: A Hobart "about 30% of our stores are in malls"; "We'll have access even within the US to urban locations that we don't have access to before with the large format stores, and we are hoping that this will be incremental to our customer base." Gupta revenue synergies "we'll share much more detailed points when the transaction has closed."
- Q Kleber GM 41bps→75bps: A Gupta 3 drivers + "two new drivers... GameChanger, as well as the DICK'S Media Network"; vertical "700 basis points to 900 basis points of higher margin." Buybacks "there are certain restrictions as we are in the phase of the S-4 filing. So we'll evaluate that appropriately for the balance of the year."
- Q Matuszewski (Jefferies) assortment trimming for tariffs: A Hobart "no, that is not a stated strategy of ours." GC free vs paid: Gupta "the 40% to 50% growth that we have been driving on a top line basis on the GameChanger platform."
- Tone: defensive on FL (stock down post-announcement); strong core; gdnc held (tariffs/macro); vertical premium restated 700-900bps (vs 600-800 Nov-2024); buybacks effectively paused.
#### 4.3 Q2 FY2025 call 2025-08-28 (Stack remote, Hobart, Gupta)
- Stack: "we delivered a very strong second quarter with comps of 5%."; "Foot Locker shareholders approved the transaction. We've also received all regulatory approvals, and we anticipate the transaction will close on September 8."
- Hobart: "We now expect comp sales growth for the year to be in the range of 2% to 3.5%, and EPS to be in the range of $13.90 to $14.50."; Q2 comps 5% "on top of a 4.5% increase last year and a 2% increase in 2023."; "in Q3, we expect to open 13 more, marking our highest number of House of Sport openings within a single quarter... which will bring our year-end total to approximately 35."; FH "approximately 15 total for the year, taking us to approximately 42 by year end."
- Gupta: "Gross profit... 37.06% of net sales and increased 33 basis points from last year. This increase was driven by a higher merchandise margin and leverage on occupancy costs due to higher sales."; "SG&A expenses increased 9.9% to $864 million and deleveraged 105 basis points."; "We now expect full year comp sales growth in the range of 2% to 3.5%, compared to our prior expectation of 1% to 3% growth. Consolidated sales are expected to be in the range of $13.75 billion to $13.95 billion"; "we expect EPS to decline year-over-year in Q3 and increase in fourth quarter."; "an effective tax rate of approximately 25%, compared to our prior expectation of approximately 24%."; "no borrowings on our new $2 billion unsecured credit facility."
- Q Nagel FL plan: A Hobart (Ed's line failed) "We plan to invest in stores, we plan to invest in marketing, and we know that there are opportunities from a core merchandising standpoint. We're excited about apparel opportunities." Tariffs/demand: "We've seen some sporadic price increases, but they are surgical and not across the board."; "There have been some selective price increases, but we and our brand partners are very surgical about when, where, and how much we can bring in some minor price increases to offset some of the tariffs."
- Q Gutman 2H comp: A Hobart "We are not seeing any signs of slowdown with the consumer."; "We did just take our comp assumptions up for the back half." Q merch margin/leverage point: A Gupta "our margins expanded 33 basis points, gross margins, and the merch margin expanded by 18 basis points"; "we believe that we can deliver a kind of a leverage on the SG&A at a low single digits comp."; "we're not sharing the long-term rubric here."
- Q Yih Nike power/price in comp: A Hobart "I don't know why I would say the balance of power would shift. I think what I would say is, our strategic relationship is very, very strong." Gupta "almost 1% of our comp came from the increase in transaction, and the remaining came from the overall basket increase."
- Q Ohmes consumer/BTS/promos: A Hobart "promotional activity hasn't been an enormous factor for us"; "we're not seeing trade-down from best to better or better to good."
- Q Lasser why GM no longer "+75bps": A Gupta "we still expect the gross margin to expand on a full year basis... we are balancing several puts and takes... between tariffs, our consistent focus on keeping our inventory really vibrant... And you can also imagine the pricing and the promotion landscapes always remains very dynamic."; "at the high end of the guidance, we still expect our operating margins to expand by 10 basis points." Q more shares for FL still accretive?: A Gupta "the shareholder election is on the 29. So, based on the consideration mix between the stock and cash, that will determine the level of accretion."
- Q Baker GC: A Hobart "we saw 7.4 million unique active users in Q2. And on average, 5.5 million monthly active users were in the app. That's up 16% year-over-year... we're on track for almost a 50% revenue growth."; synergies "We are not changing the synergy numbers. We had said $100 million to $125 million in synergies."
- Q Horvers accretion w/ ~11M shs: A Gupta "what we continue to remain confident is that this would be a accretive. But level of accretion will be depending based on the consideration mix." 2H decel: Hobart "we're also lapping second half comps that are aggressive, 50 basis points stronger than the first half... We never guide to the highest possible outcome, but we have a tremendous momentum and are just appropriately cautious as we guide." Gupta "we actually delivered a 6.4% comp in Q4."
- Q Feldman innovation/AI: A Hobart Nike running construct, licensed, trading-card tests; "early innings with AI, but there are – the tools that we are building are powered by them."
- Q Kernan footwear cycle/price/tariff COGS: A Hobart "we have not seen the consumer having any issue with the price increases." Gupta "minimal impact from tariffs in Q2. There is small impact in the second half that has been contemplated into our outlook."
- Q Civello (Truist) HoS/FH traffic; DMN scale: A Gupta "we don't break that level of details out." Hobart "we haven't broken out exactly how much we are expecting from the retail media network this year, nor next year, but I would look to this as being a long-term growth profitability driver, margin driver and revenue."
- Q Lejuez vertical/tariff pricing: A Hobart "Our vertical brands also still have 700 to 900 basis points higher margin than the average national brand. And we are the number one or two vendor in a lot of key categories for ourselves: accessories, athletic apparel, fitness, golf, team sports."
- Q Kleber vertical into FL: A Hobart "vertical brands, way too soon to tell." Gupta "we flowed through the beat on Q2 against our own internal expectations, and we have modestly raised our second half comp expectations."
- Q Cohen (Gordon Haskett) footwear concentration: A Hobart "Footwear is the engine that pulls the train. We always have said the outfit starts with the footwear." Gupta "even some of the smaller markets are able to support the House of Sport locations very, very productively."
- Tone: raised but stock pressured intraday on dropped "+75bps" GM (Lasser); close date set; tax 25%; no buybacks.
#### 4.4 Q3 FY2025 call 2025-11-25 (first combined)
- Gilch: "Earnings per diluted share results for the DICK'S Business excludes the dilutive effect of the 9.6 million shares issued as part of the Foot Locker acquisition."
- Stack: "We want to make it clear, we're doing all that our shareholders would expect us to do to make the Foot Locker Business accretive in 2026."; "Let me be candid. Foot Locker strayed from Retail 101 and did not execute the fundamentals. Post-COVID, Foot Locker did not react quickly enough when its largest brand pivoted toward a direct-to-consumer model, leaving Foot Locker with the wrong inventory, too much of what didn't sell and not enough of what did sell."; "pro forma comp sales for the full third quarter declining 4.7%, including a 10.2% decline internationally."; "Our first priority is clear. We need to clean out the garage of underperforming assets. This means clearing out unproductive inventory, closing underperforming stores, and rightsizing assets that don't align with our go-forward vision for the Foot Locker Business."; "Our intent is to get the vast majority of the inventory charges behind us by the end of the year, so we can start 2026 fresh and position Foot Locker for an inflection point during the back-to-school season in 2026."; "we expect Q4 margin rates for the Foot Locker Business to be down between 1,000 and 1,500 basis points, with pro forma Q4 comp sales being down mid- to high-single-digits."; "we've met with all of our key vendor partners, and they are fully-aligned with our vision"; "We're moving with urgency and have already kicked off an 11-store pilot"; "we expect back-to-school next year to be an inflection point"; "we continue to expect Foot Locker to be accretive to our EPS in fiscal 2026, excluding onetime costs." Leadership: Freeman (ex-Nike) FL NA; Barnes (ex-Aldi CEO) FL Intl.
- Hobart: "We now expect comp sales growth of 3.5% to 4% for the year, and EPS to be in the range of $14.25 to $14.55 for the DICK'S Business."; Q3 5.7% "on top of a 4.3% increase last year and a 1.9% increase in 2023"; "we opened 13 new House of Sport locations, the most we've ever opened in a single quarter... We now have 35 House of Sport locations nationwide"; "bringing us to a total of 42 Field House locations"; "we've launched the Collectors Club House in 20 House of Sport locations, with plans to include it in every new location going forward."
- Gupta: "Consolidated net sales increased 36.3% to $4.17 billion, driven by an approximate $931 million sales contribution from a partial quarter of owning the Foot Locker Business and a 5.7% comp increase"; "This year's consolidated results included a $46.3 million operating loss in the quarter from the Foot Locker Business"; "pro forma non-GAAP operating income for the Foot Locker Business in Q3, in its entirety, was approximately $6.8 million."; EPS bridge "non-GAAP earnings per diluted share of $2.78 for the DICK'S Business, based on a share count of 81.2 million... a $0.52 negative impact from Foot Locker operations, including the gross margin decline as well as the higher tax rate; a $0.19 negative impact from the increased share count, which was up 5.9 million, pro-rated for the eight weeks of the Foot Locker ownership."; "We expect these actions, along with other merger and integration costs, to result in a future pre-tax charge of between $500 million and $750 million."; "Excluding the onetime costs associated with our actions to address unproductive assets, we expect Q4 operating income for the Foot Locker Business to be slightly negative."; "we remain confident in achieving the previously announced $100 million to $125 million in cost synergies over the medium term, primarily from procurement and direct sourcing efficiencies."; Q4 "approximately 91 million average diluted shares outstanding... a consolidated company effective tax rate of approximately 29% for Q4."; inventory "increased 51% compared to Q3 of last year. Excluding the Foot Locker Business, inventory levels for DICK'S Business increased 2%... inventory levels for the Foot Locker Business increased approximately 5%."; "Net capital expenditures were $218 million, which included $201 million for the DICK'S Business and $17 million for the Foot Locker Business."
- Q Ohmes DKS drivers/FL closures: A Hobart "seven quarters in a row actually where we've had an over 4% comp"; "golf as well as our license business and our trading card business is really doing well." Stack "we'll give you some more guidance on that at the end of our fourth quarter call."
- Q Gutman 2026 accretion building blocks: A Stack "we gave the Foot Locker team kind of a visual that we need to clean out the garage"; "he's a Brit, and we think that EMEA truly needs to be run by a European"; "every brand has a renewed interest in being supportive to Foot Locker, and they've all talked that they want a stable and growing Foot Locker."; "One of the reasons it struggled is they haven't had access to some of the key product. They haven't had allocation of some of the product."; accretion basis "That's core Foot Locker. That's not to say we might not... We may buy back some stock. But we think from a core Foot Locker standpoint, it can be accretive to our earnings in 2026."; FL GM parity "we do know that it can be meaningfully different than it is right now. There is a huge opportunity... That product is being sold at full price."; "We just got Nike's running construct in last week... That product is being sold at full price." Gupta "the opportunity is the reverse, to say, if those stores had access to the right product and the right innovation and the newness, can those stores be turned around and made profitable."
- Q McShane markdowns: A Stack "the product that we're marking down is older product that hasn't sold"; "we don't think that the discounting is going to be meaningfully different than it was last year."
- Q Yih structural vs operational/banners/1,000-1,500bps mechanics: A Stack "we've just done an 11-store test and it was pretty capital-light... we took the inventory, most of the inventory out of the store and we re-laid out the wall"; "if you had walked into a Foot Locker store and still walk into a lot of Foot Locker stores other than these 11 and look at the wall, it's kind of merely a run-on sentence of shoes."; "there will be a program that we will sell some of this off to a jobber."; "The product that we're marking down is not key product at DICK'S Sporting Goods."
- Q Lasser accretion base/ring-fencing/Q4 decel: A Gupta "yes, the basis is on the $14.25 to $14.55 on as the basis for 2025 results." Hobart "We needed to ring-fence the DICK'S team, and DICK'S needs to stay completely focused on driving our growth and our strategic priorities, and that is exactly what we are doing."; "We just came off of a 5.7% comp and we're up against a 6.4% comp last year... the high end of our previous guidance now is the low end of our guidance."; "We don't ever guide to the best possible outcome, but we are pumped and ready to go on the DICK'S side for Q4."
- Q Baker 11-store test/why BTS: A Stack "we're bringing the apparel business back to Foot Locker"; "the third quarter or the back-to-school timeframe is the first time we will have had complete control over the assortment going forward."
- Q Wasserman 1H influence/DKS GM: A Stack "We can have some impact on Q1 and Q2 probably, hopefully, a little bit more on Q2 than Q1." Gupta "27 basis points of gross margin expansion is on top of a 70 basis points"; "we still delivered a merchandising margin expansion of 5 basis points on top of almost about a 60 basis points of positive impact last year. And there was a slight unfavorable impact from the mix... the license business performed really well, which is a fantastic growth opportunity, but has a slightly lower margin."; "We expect gross margin to expand on the back half as well as within the fourth quarter."
- Q Lejuez $500-750M split/2026 synergies: A Gupta "three main buckets. The first and foremost... is the unproductive inventory, which makes up quite a decent chunk"; store portfolio valuation; technology assets/legacy contracts; "Inventory definitely would be cash."; synergies "we'll have a better line of sight, call it, 12 weeks from now."
- Q Fernández (Telsey) merchandising/reimagined stores: A Stack "we feel that the national brands will have a meaningful increase in the apparel business in Foot Locker, which will help drive the AURs"; remove "the Kick It Club and the drop zone" for apparel space.
- Q Forbes (Guggenheim) demographics/normalized Q4: A Stack "We'll merchandise Foot Locker for Foot Locker, which is going to be a bit more basketball inspired, a bit more trend inspired, definitely more urban than the DICK'S Business. The DICK'S Business will be more sport-led along with the lifestyle product." Gupta confirms Q4 FL compare = FL normalized non-GAAP Q4 LY ($118M per Forbes).
- Tone: candid "clean out the garage"; Stack operational lead on FL; DKS gdnc raised 3rd time; new: $500-750M charge range, FL Q4 margin collapse, 29% consol tax, DKS merch margin slowed to +5bps (licensed mix, tariffs).
#### 4.5 Q4 FY2025 call 2026-03-12 (initial FY26)
- Stack: "we closed the year with another strong quarter for the DICK'S Business, delivering comps over 3% and double-digit non-GAAP EPS growth."; "We've now owned Foot Locker for about six months."; Fast Break "This is the evolution of the 11-store pilot we discussed last quarter... During Q4, our Fast Break stores drove very strong positive comps, actually meaningfully exceeding the DICK'S Business, while also delivering strong gross margin improvement. The improvement is coming from the basics: clearer storytelling, better presentation, and a more focused assortment where we removed roughly 30% of the styles on the shoe wall that were unproductive"; "we've already expanded Fast Break to an additional 10 stores in LA before the NBA All-Star Game."; "the inventory cleanup is now essentially complete... we also leveraged DICK'S value chain to efficiently clear product."; "we anticipate our closure list is now much smaller than we initially estimated."; "we expect Foot Locker to deliver growth in comp sales of between 1% to 3% and operating income in the range of $100 million to $150 million. We continue to anticipate an inflection point for both sales and profitability, beginning with the back-to-school season."
- Hobart: "we are very pleased to have delivered record sales of $14.1 billion for the DICK'S Business. Our comps increased 4.5% and exceeded the high end of our expectations... We delivered non-GAAP EPS of $14.58, also above the high end of our outlook and up from $14.05 in 2024."; "Our Q4 comps increased 3.1%, building on last year's 6.6% increase and delivering a two-year comp stack of nearly 10%."; "We opened 16 new House of Sport locations, ending the year with 35 locations nationwide. And also opened 15 new Field House locations, bringing the total to 42"; "In 2026, we plan to open approximately 14 House of Sport locations and approximately 22 Field House locations."; "We anticipate our comp sales to be in the range of 2% to 4%, which, at the midpoint, represents a 7.5% two-year comp stack. We expect operating margins for the DICK'S Business to be approximately 11.1% at the midpoint... At the consolidated company level, we expect full-year non-GAAP earnings per diluted share in the range of $13.50 to $14.50."; "we're entering one of the most compelling multiyear periods for sport in this country's history."
- Gupta: "Consolidated net sales increased 28.1% to $17.22 billion, driven by a $3.11 billion sales contribution from a partial year of owning the Foot Locker Business and a 4.5% comp increase"; "Consolidated non-GAAP earnings per diluted share were $13.20, which included just over 20 weeks of results for the Foot Locker Business and a diluted share count of 85.1 million."; Q4 "Consolidated Q4 net sales increased 59.9% to $6.23 billion, driven by a $2.18 billion sales contribution"; "pro forma comp sales for the Foot Locker Business in Q4 decreased 3.4%"; "For the DICK'S Business, gross margin expansion accelerated sequentially, increasing 67 basis points, driven entirely by higher merchandise margin"; "our actions to optimize Foot Locker's inventory... unfavorably impacted gross profit by $218 million"; "a $0.44 negative impact from higher share count due to the acquisition and a $0.16 negative impact from Foot Locker operations."; "approximately $1.35 billion of cash"; "approximately $4.91 billion of inventory... a 47% increase"; "We also repurchased 218,000 shares of our stock for $43 million at an average price of $199.51."; charges "we previously estimated and continue to expect total pre-tax charges of between $500 million and $750 million. During 2025, we recognized $390 million of these charges... Approximately $150 million of these remaining charges are expected in 2026"; "A portion of these synergy benefits are expected in 2026, which have been reflected in our outlook."
- Gupta FY26: DKS "Total sales are expected to be in the range of $14.5 billion to $14.7 billion"; "we expect slightly higher comps in the first half, driven in large part by the timing of the World Cup. Pre-opening expenses are expected to be approximately $90 million"; "we expect operating margins for the DICK'S Business to decline in the first half and expand in the second half due to the timing of the planned investments and synergy savings." FL "Total sales are expected to be in the range of $7.6 billion to $7.7 billion." Consol "full year non-GAAP operating income in the range of $1.68 billion to $1.81 billion, and the non-GAAP earnings per diluted share in the range of $13.50 to $14.50. Our earnings guidance is based on approximately 91 million average diluted shares outstanding... effective tax rate of approximately 25.5%... interest expense of approximately $70 million, and interest income to be in the range of $20 million to $25 million."; "net capital expenditure of approximately $1.5 billion"; "we plan to begin construction on approximately 18 House of Sport locations that are expected to open in 2027"; "approximately 15 Golf Galaxy Performance Center locations"; "a 3% increase in our quarterly dividend to an annualized payout of $5 per share, $1.25 on a quarterly basis. This marks the 12th consecutive year that our shareholders have benefited from a dividend increase. A 2026 plan includes our expectation for share repurchases to offset normal course dilution."
- Q Nagel DKS sales confidence/Q4 decel: A Hobart "We had a 3.1% comp growth. And importantly, we were on top of the prior year, 6.6% comp. So, on a two-year stack basis, we actually exceeded our internal expectations."; "We haven't seen trade-down."; "The two-year stack going forward is a 7.5% comp. So, 2% to 4% on top of our 4.5%."
- Q Yih cleanup/Fast Break count: A Stack "That's behind us. We cleaned out the garage with markdowns in the stores and moved product through the Foot Locker stores and the Champs stores. We also utilized... the DICK'S value chain of Going Going Gone to clean out a lot of that inventory. We were able to recover a higher cash amount"; "This inventory at Foot Locker is probably cleaner than it has ever been." Innovation: Hobart Nike run construct, signature basketball (Sabrina, A'ja, Caitlin), Gymshark "We are their first US wholesale partner."; "the new run construct from Nike doing very well. And across the board, running is really doing well. Signature basketball is also doing really, really well." Stack "Nike is doing very well. We're really pleased with them. Adidas and – we're leaning into the World Cup with Adidas"; collectibles "will be accretive to our sales number."
- Q Gutman core margin/FL EBIT lower than expected: A Stack "we actually could have kind of guided Foot Locker to be higher... based on our original projections... we're closing less stores than we had originally anticipated. If we had decided to close those stores, Foot Locker could have been a bit more profitable in Q1 and Q2"; "we will get to probably 250 of these stores by back-to-school, which is a Herculean effort." Gupta "this 67 basis points of margin expansion all came from a merchandising margin." Stack "it was more promotional out there than we had anticipated." Q 1H/2H/World Cup: A Gupta "We didn't explicitly guide to the exact number associated with it"; op margin down 1H on investments; "the synergy benefits that we are looking at will be more back-half weighted."
- Q McShane GC/DMN margin: A Hobart "it continues to drive really strong comps, like nearly 40% CAGR and strong profitability. It's a SaaS system"; DMN "has live sports in a way that really nobody else can provide." Gupta "we have the exciting opening of the six distribution center in early part of 2026. So that's contemplated in our guidance expectation."
- Q Horvers 11-store learnings/negative Q4 traffic: A Stack "took roughly 30% of the choices out of the store, relaid out the wall with the key product"; "The 250 includes the US and Europe"; "If we weren't highly confident that this Fast Break concept wouldn't be highly successful, we wouldn't be rolling out 250 of them by back-to-school." Hobart "The transactions in Q4, again, on a two-year stack basis, if you look, they were positive"; "We have been driving strong basket and AUR, and that just speaks to our differentiated product assortment. It's really not due to inflation."; "we are not concerned about traffic or transactions."
- Q Lejuez synergies beyond medium term/$700M FL OI history: A Gupta "there is a portion that is definitely in 2026 for the synergies that has been included in the guidance. And I would say, the medium term would be maybe a couple of years after that."; "In terms of the $700 million of operating income for Foot Locker, I think this is a little bit too early to be able to give a long-term outlook."
- Q Baker FL pacing/agentic: A Gupta "we expect both the sales and profitability to be back-half weighted." Hobart AI in store labor forecasting, app recommendations; agentic "More to come."
- Q Civello DMN to FL/GGG mechanics: A Hobart "a little premature... maniacally focused." Stack GGG "brings cash into the business and it cleans up the store." Gupta (GGG clearing FL inventory) "That is not contemplated in the synergies."
- Q Fernández why FL Q4 beat/regions: A Stack "the Stripers and the team at Foot Locker really getting behind the whole idea of cleaning out the garage... And we kind of came in right in line from a margin rate standpoint"; "right now, the US is a little bit ahead of Europe, but we – just because we did more of the Fast Break stores in the US than we did in Europe. But Europe is not very far behind."
- Tone: confident DKS (beat high end), cautious-constructive FL; FL 2026 OI below some expectations because fewer closures (Fast Break repositioning); first consol FY26 EPS $13.50-$14.50, 91M shs, 25.5% tax; dividend raised; buybacks reduced to dilution offset; capex ~$1.5B.
#### 4.6 Q1 FY2026 call 2026-05-27
- Stack: "Sport is one of the hottest categories in the country today. We're in the middle of a real sports moment."; "Our vision is to build the best sports company in the world, and we're just getting started."; "an exceptionally strong performance in our DICK'S Business this quarter with comps up 6%."; FL "For the global Foot Locker Business, we delivered slightly positive comps and operating income, with merch margin improvement. This marks the first quarter of positive comps for the Foot Locker Business since Q4 of 2024. Across that expanded footprint, our Fast Break stores delivered double-digit comps in Q1 and meaningful merchandise margin improvement."; "By back-to-school, we plan to have approximately 250 Fast Break stores across Foot Locker, Kids Foot Locker and Champs globally"; "These updates are fast to implement, typically completed in a few days, and require limited capital. At its core, its retail 101, and when you execute it with discipline, it works."; "This marks the first season where our team had full control over the buys"; "a bold brand relaunch."
- Hobart: Q1 6% "on top of a 4.5% increase last year and a 5.3% increase in 2024"; "We continue to see a healthy consumer across income demographics with no signs of trading down, alongside particularly strong engagement from our younger athletes."; "we are raising the low end of our expectations for comparable sales and now expect growth of 2.5% to 4%"; "At the high end of our expectations for the DICK'S Business, we now expect to drive approximately 30 basis points of operating margin expansion on a non-GAAP basis."; landlords access "including Palm Beach Gardens, Cerritos and Tysons Corner. And for the Foot Locker Business, we delivered operating income of $17.5 million, or 0.98% of net sales."; "consolidated non-GAAP income tax expense was $106.2 million, or a rate of 28.8%"; GAAP $3.54 "includes $174 million of pre-tax litigation and other settlements, partially offset by $97 million of pre-tax Foot Locker acquisition-related costs."; "Inventory was $5.42 billion... DICK'S Business inventory was up just 3%"; "We also repurchased 719,000 shares of our stock for $141 million at an average price of $196.38."; "We now expect approximately $200 million of these remaining charges in 2026, compared to our original expectation of $150 million."; "we now expect the high end of our expectation for the DICK'S Business to be approximately 11.4%, which is above our prior expectation of approximately 11.2%"; "The most significant pressure is expected in Q2, driven primarily by the timing of planned SG&A investments, including marketing tied to the World Cup and the timing of pre-opening expenses"; FL OI "$110 million to $150 million, compared to our prior expectation of $100 million to $150 million"; "approximately 90.5 million average diluted shares"; "effective tax rate of approximately 27% for the full year. This is approximately 150 basis points higher than our original expectation... unfavorably impacts our non-GAAP EPS guidance by approximately $0.25"; "We now expect net capital expenditures of approximately $1.4 billion for the full year, split roughly 70/30 across DICK'S and Foot Locker Businesses."
- Q Gutman 6% drivers/flow-through: A Hobart "This was definitely not a result of a one-time factor"; "we added 1.5 million new athletes to our database."; "we just took our high end of our guidance up 20 basis points. So, we guided to 10 basis points. We're now guiding to 30 basis points of improvement at the high end of the range, 11.4%. We're absolutely expecting leverage for the full year."
- Q Nagel FL status: A Stack "We're right on schedule with what we plan to do with Foot Locker"; "We've repaired vendor relationships with key brands that were somewhat disenchanted with Foot Locker"; "they had really lost confidence in Foot Locker, that they didn't think Foot Locker was really going to be able to kind of present their product in the way that they wanted it presented, protect their brands."; "We reduced roughly 30% of the SKU choices"; "those stores comped at over a 6% comp in the first quarter."; product "Right now, yes... the first time that we were able to buy the product and build that assortment is for the back-to-school season."
- Q McShane capex cut/Europe: A Gupta "net CapEx for the DICK'S banner to be about $1 billion for 2026 and for Foot Locker to be about $400 million"; "the decrease in our CapEx outlook by about $100 million came predominantly in the DICK'S Business." Stack "the European businesses is about where we anticipated it to be, but it's definitely a bit behind the US business. And we expect that to be that way through the end of the year."
- Q Yih macro/footwear/brand relationships: A Hobart "we've left the top end of our comp range the same, at both DICK'S and Foot Locker"; "performance running, which is doing really, really well... retro run, in particular." Stack "Foot Locker will now have a different allocation of product that they didn't have before"; "you'll see a lot of that start to come to life to even greater degree in Q1 of next year."
- Q Drbul (BTIG) DKS GM decline/basketball: A Gupta "The DICK'S gross margin climbed about 35 basis points on a year-over-year basis.
