Stack "The basketball business is coming back in a really big fashion and really built around the women's basketball business, whether that's Sabrina and A'ja."
- Q Lasser GM offsets/HoS economics: A Gupta fuel, 6th DC, occupancy, trading-card mix "contemplated"; offsets access/allocation, pricing, vertical (700-900bps), DMN, GC. Hobart trading cards "It's incremental gross margin dollars, bringing people in more frequently, appealing to a younger audience, totally incremental from the rest of our store and driving trips."; no quantification ("We do not."). HoS "we do see comp store growth in years three and years four even, so we've been able to confirm that. So they open fully and then continue to grow."; "spending significantly higher than an average a typical fixed athlete"; Bioré and Gymshark came in through HoS; "our Field House concept, which is our 50k prototype, is really a mini version of a House of Sport."
- Q Lejuez good/better/best; leverage/repo: A Hobart DSG opening price point through technical. Gupta "we bought $140 million of shares already in Q1 and still finished the quarter one with $1 billion of cash on the balance sheet. So, we have plenty of flexibility. And from a share repurchase perspective, I would say we'll continue to be opportunistic." Hobart "Maxfli is doing amazingly well"; "700 basis points to 900 basis points higher in margin."; "Within hardlines, feeling really terrific about team sports and licensed and trading cards and golf."
- Q Horvers tax stimulus/FL AUR/buy-in synergies: A Gupta "I don't know if I would call out that we saw any significant benefit from the stimulus checks." Stack FL txns not reported until comp in Q4; "there was certainly still some clearance"; buy-in synergies in FL Q1 GM: "No, no."
- Q Fernández Fast Break pace/BTS: A Stack "We will continue that program through holiday"; "better assortment for women's product... basketball... performance run, but also the retro run category... better apparel product."
- Q Civello GLP-1/promos: A Hobart "we don't have any specific correlation to GLP-1s"; "In Q1, we didn't – that wasn't a major factor."
- Tone: most bullish in set; 6% DKS comp, FL first positive comp, both low ends raised, DKS high-end margin 11.4%; offsets tax 27%, 2026 charges $200M, DKS Q1 GM down, capex $1.4B; consol EPS held, not raised.
#### 4.7 Q2 FY2026 call 2026-08-25 (gdnc cut)
- Stack: "From a sales perspective, the second quarter was strong, particularly for the DICK'S Business, which delivered nearly a 5% comp sales gain."; "as the quarter progressed, it became clear that inventory levels were building up across parts of the industry, leading to a much more promotional environment. This pressured our overall company earnings."; "As demand continued to shift during the quarter, inventory built up in parts of the industry, particularly within certain legacy footwear silhouettes and apparel franchises that simply aren't resonating the way they once did."; "we felt it was important to remain competitively priced to protect our leadership position... we view the pricing investments we're making today as an investment in the future strength of our business."; FL "given its greater exposure to many of the legacy footwear silhouettes, the impact was more significant at Foot Locker. In addition, Foot Locker is more dependent on launch and retro product, not only were there fewer launches in the second quarter, but launches we did see performed below industry and our expectations."; "We expect the broader promotional environment, particularly around legacy silhouettes, to remain challenging at least through the fourth quarter."; "the Foot Locker Business in EMEA has been more challenging than expected... The promotional environment remains very aggressive in EMEA, the industry is carrying too much inventory, and the consumer has been even more cautious than expected due to the geopolitical environment."; "its first major brand campaign in more than a decade"; "We surpassed our back-to-school goal of approximately 250 Fast Break stores globally and will continue to expand to more locations ahead of the holiday season."; "Our brand partners continue to be highly supportive of both the DICK'S and Foot Locker Businesses, and we believe they see us as the most important player in the global athletic ecosystem."; "We've navigated environments like this before and we remain confident in our strategy, our competitive position, and long-term opportunities ahead."
- Hobart: "In Q2 we delivered total sales growth of 5.6% and comp sales growth of 4.9% for the DICK'S Business. Importantly, our growth outpaced the broader industry by nearly 200 basis points"; "The DICK'S Business also delivered gross margin expansion during the quarter."; "the contributions from these businesses [DMN, GameChanger] plus tariff refunds recognized during the quarter helped offset the promotional pressure"; "We invested significantly in marketing around the event [World Cup], primarily through our adidas partnership"; "During Q2, we opened five House of Sport locations and eight Field House locations. For the year, we expect to open approximately 14 total House of Sport locations and 20 total Field House locations."; "Our ScoreCard loyalty program is one of the most powerful assets in the DICK'S Business, connecting us with approximately 30 million active athletes."; "we introduced ScoreCard+, a paid-tier designed for our most engaged athletes, who will pay $99 per year for membership and enhanced loyalty offerings."; "nothing we've seen changes our strategy or our confidence in the DICK'S Business, and our sales expectations remain unchanged."
- Gupta: "Consolidated net sales increased 53.2% to $5.59 billion, driven by a $1.74 billion contribution from the Foot Locker Business and a 4.9% comp increase"; "Pro forma comps for Foot Locker Business declined 3.6%"; "Within the DICK'S Business, gross margin expanded 79 basis points versus last year. The improvement was driven by strong growth in DICK'S Media Network and GameChanger, as well as the benefit from tariff refunds"; "we received approximately $59 million of tariff refunds, including $57 million related to DICK'S Business and $2 million related to the Foot Locker Business. Of the total amount, a benefit of approximately $21 million was included in our non-GAAP results for Q2, and $38 million was excluded as one-time benefit"; "We have reinvested these benefits into the business to remain competitively priced"; "for the DICK'S Business, SG&A deleveraged 96 basis points, driven by our strong investments in World Cup marketing... In addition, we are experiencing higher teammate healthcare costs."; "operating income of $485.2 million or 12.6% of net sales for DICK'S Business, and an operating loss of $31.9 million for the Foot Locker Business."; GAAP $3.50 "includes approximately $40 million of pre-tax income related to the tariff refunds and approximately $29 million of pre-tax Foot Locker acquisition-related costs. It also includes approximately $15 million of costs associated with redesigning the store labor model."; "approximately $914 million of cash"; "Inventory for the DICK'S Business was up 6%, in line with our total sales growth."; charges "we expect total pre-tax charges of up to $750 million. To-date, we have recognized $516 million of these charges."; gdnc "While we continue to expect full year comp sales growth in the range of 2.5% to 4%, we now expect operating margins in the range of 10.6% to 10.9%, compared to our prior expectation of 11% to 11.4%. For full year, we now expect gross margin to decline slightly. This reflects our expectation for a more promotional marketplace through the balance of the year, as well as higher expected fuel prices and supply chain expenses."; "we expect gross margin pressure to be most pronounced in Q3. We also expect SG&A expenses to deleverage for the full year, including, at the midpoint, nearly 50 basis points of deleverage in Q3"; FL "full year pro forma comp sales to be in the range of negative 2% to flat compared to our prior expectation of 1.5% to 3% growth. We now expect an operating loss for the Foot Locker Business in the range of $80 million to $40 million compared to our prior expectations of $110 million to $150 million in profit."; "full year non-GAAP earnings per diluted share in the range of $11 to $12 compared to our prior range of $13.50 to $14.50"; "approximately 90 million average diluted shares"; "effective tax rate of approximately 29% for the full year. This is approximately 200 basis points higher... unfavorably impacts our non-GAAP EPS guidance by approximately $0.35"; capex "approximately $1.4 billion... split roughly 70/30."; "DICK'S Media Network and GameChanger, that they will start to become a bigger driver of the merch margin expansion as well."
- Q Gutman what changed in 90 days: A Stack "The DICK'S stores comped 4.9% and we did not take our sales expectations down on the DICK'S side."; "What changed is a number of brands got very promotional on their sites, and those promotions spilled into the broader marketplace; and we expect that to, unfortunately, continue through the balance of the year."; "DICK'S is not nearly as reliant on the footwear business as Foot Locker is."; "we really believe that that's one of the best investments we can make in this business, is to keep that leadership position and not give that market share"; "another thing from a Foot Locker standpoint that we didn't realize would continue to be as difficult as it is is EMEA"; "what we did with Foot Locker is absolutely the long-term right decision, although, right now, I'm sure some of you are kind of scratching your head, but we absolutely believe long-term, it was the right thing to do."
- Q Gutman footwear fatigue vs supplier clearance: A Stack "I don't think this is a demand issue going forward from a footwear standpoint"; "I don't think this is a demand issue. The consumer is looking for products that are new, innovative, different in the marketplace and some of these older legacy silhouettes and franchises that have done so well have slowed and slowed relatively quickly."; "whether it's from the Nike Mind shoe, the Nike running construct is great. The adidas women's product in print and pattern has done extremely well, but I think this is temporary."; apparel "the Nike Solo Fleece... Gymshark... Free People Movement's doing extremely well."
- Q Horvers cycle hangover/FL 2H cadence: A Stack "I think we have the hangover right now. We're going through that with these legacy silhouettes."; "Nike changed the running silhouette. They really redefined this whole category of business. They've done extremely well with the Vomero and Peg."; "take a look at what's going on with UGG and Birkenstock. We couldn't be happier with what's going on with those styles of shoes."; "We think Q3 is going to be a bit more difficult than Q4"; "I've never seen the specialty channel of distribution so promotional in my career" (quoting a brand); "you need roughly five pairs of shoes... the footwear business is not going away." Hobart "the investment in margin and SG&A on the DICK'S side also will be more aggressive in Q3 rather than Q4."; "the running category in itself and all performance is doing really well... this is focused on the lifestyle legacy silhouettes."
- Q McShane Fast Break vs legacy/count/EMEA share of cut: A Stack "the Fast Break stores have definitely outperformed the traditional legacy stores"; "it'll be somewhere in the north of 300, 350 doors by the end of the year globally"; "EMEA is much more impactful than in the US."; "The impact on EMEA is much greater than what's going on in the US. It's a more competitive market, it's a much more promotional market than the US right now."
- Q Yih lifestyle vs performance/brown shoe/spring orders: A Stack "I do think there is a shift toward this brown shoe piece of this, whether it's UGG and Birkenstock... Those businesses for us are really on fire."; "on the DICK'S side, we think the footwear business is going to comp positively in the back half of the year on the DICK'S side, although there will be some margin pressure"; "we're modifying some orders here and there but nothing really out of the ordinary."
- Q Hutchinson (BofA) FL self-help on margin: A Stack "we're not leading this margin erosion. We are participating in it where we have to make sure that we keep our market share, but we are in no way leading the price issues here." Hobart apparel into FL stores + new-product allocations help.
- Q Boruchow (Wells) BTS read; $200M rev cut → $200M profit cut: A Stack sales cut, margin change, plus marketing: "Foot Locker hasn't had an out-of-home marketing plan since roughly 2013"; campaign "called Colors"; "We've also added payroll to our Stripers."; BTS "we don't give any guidance in – inter-quarter guidance of how back-to-school is doing."
- Q Lasser 2027 economics/FL rightsizing: A Hobart "the 4.9% comp that the DICK'S Business had in Q2 does include World Cup. But even if you exclude the World Cup, we did have growth across the portfolio." Gupta "Our merch margin expanded almost about 150 basis points. That included the benefits of the tariff... even if you back that out, we were very happy that we were able to not only drive the margin expansion from these new capabilities, but offset the promotional pressures." Rightsizing/monetizing FL assets: Stack "we're not going to kind of lay out specifics, but the answer to your question is simply yes." Gupta "we reiterated our confidence in terms of the synergies of $100 million to $125 million, and that's the bigger part of the rubric as well that we all feel confident about."; "we feel like the rubric that we have talked about of being able to continue to drive gross margin expansion and making appropriate level of investments is the right framework, and we will continue to leverage that as we go into 2027."
- Q Lejuez SG&A/FL marketing timing: A Stack "I don't think there's a better time to do this right now when the market is in a bit of a turmoil from a marketing standpoint to gain more market share." Hobart "We are building an entire ecosystem here, and they are important parts of it, so absolutely not considering slowing down." Gupta "we expect the SG&A to deleverage for the full year with the highest pressure in the third quarter... to the tune of about 50 basis points. And then as we start to lap the investments that we made in fourth quarter, we expect SG&A to leverage."
- Q Drbul trading cards: A Stack "We're building the Collectors Clubhouse in all House of Sport stores going forward. We're moving that into a number of the Field House locations"; partnership "with Michael Rubin and his company."
- Stack: "every once in a while, an industry has to go through a little bit of pain to reset."
- Tone: sharp reversal from May; DKS sales held but margin/GM cut; FL guided profit → loss; consol EPS cut ~$2.50 at mid; tax up again; Stack framing = industry inventory/legacy-silhouette "hangover", deliberate price investment to hold share, continued FL brand/Striper investment; FH FY plan 22 → 20; first mention ScoreCard+ ($99/yr), tariff refunds, store-labor-model redesign, higher healthcare costs.

Sales $8.8B FY19 → $14.1B FY25 (+61%, +$5.42B); market share ~7% → ~9%.

### 2. Channel-check grid (every quantitative claim; quote exact)
Sell-in May-Jul 2026 y/y:
- E1 +2%: "we are trending at a slight increase of 2%, which is behind on the posted growth, obviously, that DICK'S as a entire business is posting"
- E2 n/a new account; outperforming other accounts 10-15%: "Cannot give you a sense, but they're doing 10%-15% better."
- E3 0-5%: "The percentage change, I'm going to give ranges, has been 0%-5% growth, which does not seem incredibly strong."
- E4 −9% blended; HEYDUDE +12%, Crocs −19%: "The sandals/clog business is a little bit bigger. Overall, we're down about 9% year over year between the two brands." / "we're down about 19% year-over-year" / "we are up 12% year-over-year"
- E4 booking swings Q1 −23%, Q2 +40%; this qtr "down about 40%" pre at-once; ~40% at-once account: "Q1 2025 versus Q1 2026, we were down 23%, but we saw a major lift in Q2 of 2026, up more than 40% year over year for that timeframe." / "We're down about 40% year over year during this quarter, but we haven't seen any of their at-once orders. They're about a 40% at-once account."
- E5 receipts MSD-HSD: "What we have seen is that receipts have been up in the mid-to-high single-digit range."
- E6 +10-12%: "we've still been planning up in the +10% to +12% range."
- E7 low-mid SD: "I would say we had probably low to mid-single digit growth."
- E8 from zero: "Definitely an increase because we were not in DICK'S Sporting Goods last year."
- E9 triple digits off tiny base: "Easily triple digits, but we were on a basis of practically nothing."
- E10 flat planned: "We stayed flat on purpose. We were planned flat."
- E11 "Business was up conservatively."
- E12 +3-5%: "I think it's about 3%-5% increase overall."
- E13 +238%: "our increase with them was 238% on sell-in, and our sell-through is in line with sell-in."
- E14 +4-6%: "We are seeing 4%-6% increases."
Forecast Aug-Oct 2026 y/y:
- E1 +2-5%: "we will, again, post a growth from somewhere between 2%-5% on our commodity categories."
- E2 −5-10% post-WC: "I would say it will slow down by 5%-10%, but it's natural. The hype is gone."
- E3 0-5%: "it will continue in the next quarter to be in the 0%-5% growth zone."
- E4 Q4 (as answered) ~−10% blended; Crocs −9%; HEYDUDE −80% before pre-books: "I think on the Crocs side, we'll be down about 9% in Q4. On the boat shoes brand side, right now, we're down 80%, but we still have pre-book orders to come in as well as their at-once business. I'm assuming we'll be down probably 10% when it's all said and done."
- E5 MSD-HSD: "we are really thinking mid-single digits to high single digits from an increase standpoint."
- E6 ~+10%: "About a 10% increase over last year."
- E7 "sizable", pull-forward to Aug: "there's a lot of pull forward and demand into August, so we're going to see a lot of growth in those three months you're referencing."
- E9 flat to slightly up SD: "we are going to be flat or projecting to be flat to slightly up in the single digits versus last year"
- E10 flat: "Flat. We will be flat to last year."
- E11 positive, no %: "I can't give a percentage change. It's a little early for that sell-in expectation. I think from a North America perspective, we are always expected to be in the positive."
- E12 +10-15%, half from doors: "I would assume there is about a 10%-15% increase. Half of that is due to increased number of doors."
- E13 fall +150%: "We've increased the sell-in to DICK'S for the upcoming fall season by approximately 150%. That's not necessarily a wider assortment, but more pairs per style that we're giving them."
- E14 +14-16%: "We expect anywhere from 14%-16% increase over prior year."
CY2026 sell-in:
- E1 ~+5% back-loaded Q4: "our expectation is that we will see a growth versus the prior year of roughly 5%, but it will be heavily backloaded towards the last quarter"
- E3 0-5%: "the balance of this year is going to be in the 0%-5% zone."
- E4 +4%: "Yeah, it's 4% higher."
- E5 HSD: "Really seeing a positive high single-digit growth rate."
- E6 +10%: "Full year, we're looking for 10% increase over LY in sell-in shipments"
- E7 LSD: "I'd say low single digits calendar year 2026 versus calendar year 2025."
- E9 >100%: "the growth is over 100%, as I mentioned, on a very small base."
- E10 up slightly vs flat plan: "We're up slightly, although we did plan the year flat."
- E12 +10-15%: "I would say a rough guess between 10%-15% increase from last."
- E13 +150%: "We will take the DICK'S business overall up 150%, which is significantly greater than the amount of increase that we are allowing most retailers to have."
- E14 double digit, 4-20% by category: "We're seeing double-digit increase in current forecast sell-in, and depending on the category, could be anywhere from 4%-20%."
Order behavior:
- E7 pull-forward: "they are just trying to front-load next season as much as possible, so we're just seeing aggressive replan orders and trying to pull forward orders right now."
- E1 (supplier side) moved away from front-loading: "Last year, we made the decision to rely less on front-loading our order book and doing more in-season management."
- E6 fall set orders +10-12%: "they're setting a little bit larger than they did a year ago, so our set orders right now are up in that 10%-12% range."
- E4 Q4 pre-books men's HEYDUDE ~+100%: "the men's business remaining about flat with the exception of Q4 pre-books, which were up nearly 100% in the men's business."; allocation increased twice for college football & NHL: "We've had to increase allocations for them twice now for both programs."; drop ship +12% YTD → ~+100% by YE: "We have seen a year-over-year increase of 12% to this point. That's all at-once business. I anticipate that that volume is probably going to be close to 100% increase year-over-year by year's end."
Pricing/tariffs/cost:
- E12 pinnacle +$50, staples +$10: "the top pinnacle product is the one that is driving the costs, whether it be 50 increases, where our everyday staple products, $10 or so is usually increased to help with margins"
- E11 embellishment upcharge brand $15 vs retailer $5: "We're like, 'Oh, that should be a 15 upcharge.' Retailers will push back and say, 'Actually, we believe it should only be a $5 upcharge.'"
- E12 tariffs refunded/stable: "because the tariffs were returned and people are coming through and seeing that, and most companies did not raise their prices too high, I feel as though that the pricing right now is going to stay stable."
- E11 leveling out: "I think we've gotten past things such as tariffs affecting things like pricing and costing. I feel like that is leveling out a bit. We're not seeing that as much this year. That more impacted the business last year."
- E13 biggest cost input: "Currently, it would be tariffs."
- E2 "Tariff is a little bit slowing down as a risk. We might have a little bit of transport cost linked to the oil crisis."
- E11 macro: "Every time gas prices raise $0.10, that then in turn takes away about $10 billion globally of consumer spending."
Margin/markdown asks:
- E3 none this qtr: "What you start to usually see is they start asking for additional returns outside their normal allowance. That has not been something that has been happening in this current quarter, or they may ask for markdown dollars, and that has not been occurring in this current quarter as well."
- E2 WC closeout: "they're pushing for sure for more money to close out in case some World Cup styles will be obsolete."
- E7 "I think they just would love to see some increased margin opportunity on just specific products."
- E10 "They look for a specific margin on the way in that we need to hit"
- E13 "they are taking markdowns when product goes off of MAP with us, and we do not have margin agreements in place, so there's not a conversation of them asking us to support that markdown."
- E3 tighter from FL leverage: "I would say tighter negotiation pressure due to the purchase of Foot Locker. It starts to eliminate who you can use as leverage"
Inventory:
- E10 swim ~5% overbought: "It is in swim, and it is roughly 5%."
- E12 DKS rising, FL a hit: "we currently see an upward trend within the inventory sector for them. To be honest, from an outsider looking in, that Foot Locker inventory must have been a pretty big hit for them."
- E2 (cites DKS +3% May indirectly): "This is also visible by the very good Q1 results that they have registered in the stock and cash part during the latest release of results."
- E4 supplier excess: "we had more excess inventory at this time than we did last year."
- Lead times 6-9 months (E1, E3, E5, E13): "we place the order six to nine months in advance" / "we take orders six to seven months in advance" / "Our buys are actually placed nine or so months in advance" / "our retailers buy product from us approximately eight months ahead of delivery"
- E1 replenishment: "the delivery of goods to DICK'S upon reorder of two to three days maximum."
Doors:
- E12 "I think they have maybe about 10-15 new DICK'S House of Sport. ... I think it is around 18-25 Field House locations opening up this year."; "I think they are increasing, 15-ish House of Sport, 20 more Field House locations, plus number of other regular DICK'S Sporting Goods."
- E13 "Very little from new doors. It's primarily deeper penetration in existing doors as we've increased their allocation."
- E14 FL SKU reduction: "SKU reduction of about 20%-30%, which isn't dropping revenue expectations, it's just offering a cleaner and tighter assortment."
Share of wallet:
- E12 Nike share: "half of their sales, to 30% of DICK'S Sporting Goods sales in footwear, are all Nike."; apparel: "We actually know that the apparel sector is really growing for DICK'S Sporting Goods right now. I think they talked about maybe a 25%-30% increase in their sales" (both unverified; former internally inconsistent per compiler)
- E5 base layer: "We have been seeing continuous double-digit increases on double-digit increases on double-digit increases."; premium fleece slowing: "If you look back a year or two ago at $120, $130, $40, $50 price points, definitely seeing softness on that side of the business."
HoS/FL/promo/other:
- E11 HoS: "Those locations are driving quite a bit of business, especially new non-comp business for them. As well as the fact that those types of locations get the most exclusive product"
- E13 "I think the primary success for them in store has been in those House of Sport doors."
- E5 "their retail experience, especially when you think about their House of Sport doors, has really been pretty unmatched. Really, SCHEELS is the only other retailer that has a similar experience, similar space."
- E3 "My hypothesis is that the two combined will purchase less than historically they have than when they were on their own."
- E2 Fast Break may order more socks: "Yes, they might order more stock."
- E11 "I'd say on the Foot Locker business, Retro driving that side of the business, and again, being a bright spot."; "On the Foot Locker side, I think we're seeing an increase in door penetration where we have apparel."
- E5 "DICK'S and the marketplace at large are definitely getting more promotional. There are more discounts to the consumer."
- E8 "From the outside, just looking at their marketplace, it does not seem that they are overly promotional"
- E7 "We had a change in our promotion cadence, so we had an extremely large K87. It's a T-shirt for us during Father's Day that significantly outperformed expectations"
- E4 supplier-funded discounts: "we're offering to our largest wholesale partners a discount to help try to grow and establish brand awareness and recognition for both our sandals and boot silhouettes"
- E2 "I think that we have to produce at least one good innovation per year."
- E14 "through a product life cycle from concept to consumer-facing is about 18 months."
- E13 "We have top five to seven styles that drive the majority of our business from a volume perspective."

### 3a. FL integration as seen by vendors
- E1: "Since prior, Foot Locker was also a customer of ours in slightly different categories as well as in commodity categories. They too would buy a different assortment. The way we see it is just a name on the order sheet that changes. It's not necessarily the buying behavior of the group that changes."
- E2: "They have a very strong one, which is driven also by the inclusion of the acquired Foot Locker business, and Foot Locker's tools and shoes are linked to socks."; Fast Break: "They try to sell proxy categories. For sure, socks, it is a major proxy category to see."
- E3: "part of the challenge with them is with the purchase of Foot Locker, there is going to be some consolidation of stores, most likely, some rationalization of the assortment, etc."; "Both store rationalization and assortment overlap."; "as DICK'S continues to streamline from purchasing Foot Locker, the big question is going to be what does that do? The answer most likely is gives them a lot more negotiation power when it comes to purchasing and pricing, which is not necessarily good for any of the brands that do business with DICK'S because they're going to have a huge command of the marketplace, in the U.S. at least. That's why I think it would be really good in the U.S. if JD Sports continues to build out their presence to give a little competition in that area."
- E5: "From a DICK'S Sporting Goods standpoint, it is not changing any of their ordering patterns or quantities or styles. Their run is pretty separate organization, so we are not seeing any buy from one affecting buy from the other."; "The market, the industry is better with a strong Foot Locker, so excited for them to continue to find success and also roll that out as quickly as possible"
- E11: "I think the biggest thing that's driving it, again, is them buying Foot Locker. That was a huge revenue shift for them altogether. Now being able to plan that business, we have this four- to five-year plan right now"; "with that acquisition of Foot Locker, that's been huge for them, especially in terms of tightening up their strategy and increasing their apparel strategy as well. Foot Locker had historically only been focused on footwear."; Fast Break gravitates to: "It's going to be apparel."
- E12: "We do know that they have been consolidating and moving product within their channels to liquidate, go through there. We, as a partner, have not seen any dips or changes in any ordering from DICK'S Sporting Goods."; "They flashed last quarter how high it was when we dove into it. It was mostly because of Foot Locker, so we feel very confident after that kind of dive in to see how they're going. They're right-sizing that."; "As you may or may not know, some malls and locations have a DICK'S and a Foot Locker nearby. It's wondering what type of product lands in one of those stores so they don't cannibalize each other"; Fast Break: "Hopefully, it becomes an even playing field where sadly with the strong investment in Nike, we feel as though that is going to be very Nike-forward moving forward."; "Overall, I think it's strengthened because of the clarity that we are getting from Foot Locker. That was the biggest one that we were concerned about."
- E14: "They have done an incredible job of editing to amplify, meaning they have really decluttered the space for Foot Locker, which has strengthened our ability to grow the business with DICK'S Sporting Goods."; "Aggressively in regards to the acquisition of Foot Locker. They've aggressively cleaned up their inventory position and their breadth of product with the Foot Locker doors."; "Yes, it's cleaned up their ordering process, so they're more strategic as far as the transformation strategy, which includes that edit to amplify, the SKU reduction, to really clean up that shoe wall and highlight key volume drivers."; (Top-15 variant) "We're seeing that DICK'S is really working towards editing to amplify, so SKU reduction of about 20%-30%..."
- E8: "I don't know what that means. We are not currently working with Foot Locker, so I do not know if that is relevant." E13 "Not at this point."; E10 "No, not in our categories."; E6 "Not at this point. We haven't seen any changes to what they're ordering based on the format."
- Compiler: 9 of 14 reported no change to own orders from Fast Break; FL-exposed vendors (E11, E12, E14) described SKU cuts 20-30%, Nike-forward allocation, Retro as FL bright spot, apparel push into FL doors; no vendor reported FL comp data or EMEA; only negative FL volume view = E3 "buys less" hypothesis.

### 3b. Footwear cycle / Nike / brands
- E3: "performance running as a category for a lot of folks, but for Nike as well, has been on fire and doing extremely well. That, combined with also what we call retro running, so the bringing back of old retro styles and reintroducing them, has also been doing incredibly well."; "Sportswear is tough. The consumer decides, and it is trendy. I would say sportswear continues to be a challenge. The consumer outside of running is spending a little money on brown shoes like BIRKENSTOCKs. I would say flip-flops and sandals are making a comeback."; "HOKA and On continue to do extremely well. BIRKENSTOCK continues to do extremely well. All the running brands, ASICS, Brooks, New Balance, are doing very, very well."; "coming off of a year, the prior year in the same quarter, with a lot of cleanup to do, and resetting the marketplace, at least for our brand, this felt pretty good as like a return to growth for DICK'S."
- E5: "Nike seems like it has been a mixed bag lately, where it has been getting better, but it has definitely been some hits and misses. When you think about inventory levels and footwear, it has definitely been relatively slow, especially on the lifestyle side, which historically has been their bread and butter."; "Seeing a lot of success around Adidas right now, especially in the World Cup year, both on the apparel and footwear side."; "I am seeing some of the smaller brands like New Balance, Asics, still continue to do well for DICK'S Sporting Goods, even if we are hearing of softness from different accounts, especially when you think about the mall retailers like a Foot Locker, JD."
- E7: "I can't speak for DICK'S, but I feel like there's a softening of brands in general. Companies like Nike, North Face, Patagonia don't have the brand power that they used to."
- E11: "Primary drivers are going to be footwear, number one, is driving the business and looks very healthy, especially from things like our basketball shoe franchise. Them being up within that franchise is hitting the right price points for their consumers."; "While we still have the most coveted launches within footwear, and even sometimes apparel in the industry, other competitors are taking our exact playbook and doing the same exact thing. It's a land grab right now"; "Easy one would be within our Retro or AJ1 portfolio. When it's the right Retro product or AJ1 product, that will definitely drive demand at DICK'S Sporting Goods."; "If it's a product that's maybe a higher-price point, maybe it shouldn't go full doors, again, because they have got those core doors where that consumer is going to expect probably a lower-price point. They're not going to be willing to spend $180 on a pair of shoes."
- E12: "We've noticed that there have been a big shift with DICK'S Sporting Goods right now, especially in the run category. They've been really investing in trying to target that run community within the likes of HOKA, On, Nike, and Adidas. We've been seeing that big shifts and a downward shift when it comes to lifestyle product"; "The biggest elephant in the room when it comes to competition is the renewed relationship that DICK'S Sporting Goods has with Nike. ... The reinvestment that Nike has because they went into their D2C environment was very helpful for smaller brands like HOKA and On to kind of break into DICK'S Sporting Goods. Now, with the reinvestment that Nike has in DICK'S Sporting Goods, that's going to be a bit of a worry for other brands"; "Right now, we're looking at stability shoes as that big movement that everyone's going for. ... the everyday walker, the everyday consumer, the one that wants to run and walk with their shoes, maybe even take it to work, that's really targeting that DICK'S Sporting Goods consumer."; "I think the only time DICK'S Sporting Goods will lose market share, and I'm going to say it's a very small portion of it, is with run specialties."
- E13: "there's more caution in the sneaker category with sneakers overall showing some slowness in the marketplace, particularly on fashion sneakers, and that's where we fall in versus true performance athletics, which are extremely strong still."; "We haven't changed it with everyone, but we have increased allocation for DICK'S because they have shown to be tremendous partners to us."
- E14: "There's a very strong Crocs business, BIRKENSTOCK, but UGG brand is continuing to drive the majority of the sales."
- E9: "Across our greater brand portfolio that does not sell to DICK'S, we have seen a slowdown in sneakers"
- E4 slowing: "It's our sneaker business. It is our unisex business on the casual sandals and clogs side."; allocation: "No. We basically have three tiers of allocation, so DICK'S would be in that top tier to get allocation ahead of the second and third tier."
- Compiler: convergence on performance running (HOKA, On, ASICS, Brooks, NB, Nike running) + brown-shoe/comfort (UGG, Birkenstock, Crocs) strong; lifestyle/fashion sneakers & legacy sportswear slow (E12, E5, E13, E4); Nike "mixed", "slow on lifestyle" (E5) but reinvesting in DKS (E12); DKS top-tier allocation (Crocs, UGG); no one quantified DKS allocation vs FL/JD/Academy.

### 3c. HoS / Field House / store growth
- E11: "they've been increasing their House of Sport locations. Those locations are driving quite a bit of business, especially new non-comp business for them. As well as the fact that those types of locations get the most exclusive product, not just from our brand but across all of the brands. They carry a much different product portfolio than their core DICK'S Sporting Goods stores."
- E12: "I think they have maybe about 10-15 new DICK'S House of Sport. Those opening up definitely helps increase the amount of spend that they have. I think it is around 18-25 Field House locations opening up this year."; "Half of that is due to increased number of doors." (on 10-15% Aug-Oct); "the expansion of doors, especially with House of Sport, which I think is an amazing opportunity for any brand to get into, really showcases pinnacle product that DICK'S Sporting Goods really highlights and backs into, and they back it with inventory, and they back it with dollars."; "One thing that we have been eyeing because of the accessory space is their Lids shop-in-shop."
- E10: "Merchandising, specifically in their DICK'S House of Sport, the way that they present product. Our shop in shops in their stores really help quite a bit."; "their private-label business is expanding and the store presence that they have with their private-label business in specifically their DICK'S House of Sport is pretty impressive."
- E13: "It was primarily focused on the House of Sport doors, but our increase with them was 238% on sell-in"; "I think the concept of head to toe and things look extremely fresh. The marketplace is pretty volatile right now, and there really needs to be a reason to buy."
- E14: "What is helping right now is DICK'S Sporting Goods' strategy with their House of Sport, Field House, and their innovation store concepts."; "They are very strategic on placing pinnacle and premium in key markets where they can drive a higher price point while also protecting the core. We are seeing them take risks in strategic doors and markets while also maintaining core growth within the balance of their doors."
- E8: "I know that they are focused on House of Sport, which is very unique to DICK'S Sporting Goods. That one, they've added stores this year, so that's really exciting."; "Looking at their public results, they are expanding doors, which is huge. To me, that signals a general strong performance and that they are profitable"
- E9: "they have begun rolling out our merchandise in a slow fashion. There's definitely an opportunity to be doing this more aggressively"
- Compiler: HoS gets most exclusive product, bigger buys, heavier marketing; 3 footwear vendors attribute most 2026 DKS growth to HoS/FH placement or new doors; E12's 10-15 HoS/18-25 FH brackets mgmt plan (~14 HoS, ~22 FH Mar-2026 release; ~20 FH Aug-2026 call).

### 3d. Vendor terms & margin
Pricing stance:
- E1: "I would honestly say they're becoming more stable with DICK'S" / "We would expect some tougher negotiations, especially on margins."
- E5: "Pricing with us and from a wholesale brand relationship is the same. We work on not necessarily a fixed margin, but we have established trade terms, so there aren't a ton of changes there."
- E6: "Prices have been a little bit choppy as the tariffs and the gas prices, transportation is elevated, and we are all having to eat some of that cost, the supplier, the retailer, and the end consumer" / "everybody wants to keep the prices flat."
- E7: "They are more stable." / "Probably a little bit more pressure, but I would say we are in a much better place than we were last year."
- E8: "They don't seem to be price-point driven. They offer a wide range of prices across all their different types of brands."
- E9: "We have a product right now that is highly in demand, so there is not a lot of negotiating happening on that front. However, should the trend change, I imagine that the pressure would increase."
- E4: "They're always looking for additional discount." / "I'd say it's consistent with a year ago."
- E13: "They're stable, but we are a MAP brand, and DICK'S has no problem working within our MAP requirements."
- E14: "We are actually seeing pricing structure more favorable with DICK'S Sporting Goods. They are not seeing the consumer needing to trade down. ... We are not seeing any price point deterioration." / "Tighter negotiation margin requirements will also be a conversation every season with our key retail partners."
- E12: "At this time, DICK'S has not been aggressive on chasing pricing and margins to where we feel concerned about."
Tariffs/cost inputs:
- E1: "Definitely, the tariffs and the instability that comes with them. Definitely, the cost of freight, which has gone up... As well as the rise in raw material costs, but also a rise in labor costs in some of the territories that we produce in, which is mostly in the Far East."
- E4: "The vast majority of our manufacturing has been moved outside of tariff-implemented countries. However, there is a major cost associated with getting all that product moved" / "the brands that were late to the party in addressing this and in moving product outside of countries like China... are the ones who are really struggling right now and really having to take massive margin hits."
- E5: "All the above. Hold the tariffs. Freight is more expensive. A war or two going on in the Middle East still, and Russia and the Ukraine driving up oil prices."
- E7: "Shipping has been a huge challenge for us the past few months with the war in Iran and the Strait of Hormuz being impacted by that, and so shipping costs have been a moving target."
- E12: "Everyone started passing costs off, and costs are not going down."
- E13: "Freight, tariffs, and material." / "Currently, it would be tariffs."
- E9: "As the cost of petroleum increases, so too does our cost to make our product, and at times, that cost increase does have to be passed along to the retailer."
Markdown money/chargebacks (see §2 for E3, E2, E7, E13, E10 items):
- E4: "They're always one of the more, for lack a better term, we'll call it strict accounts as far as chargebacks go. We haven't seen a massive increase from them year over year"
- E11: "You can also have some RTVs. Those are essentially return to vendor. I see a little stickiness like that."
- E13: "No, the only changes that have gone on with them have been warehouse changes, like shifting from one warehouse to another. No change with the order book, with the quantity on any purchase order, or any chargebacks."
- E1 (chargebacks/compliance): "Yes, there have been, but not of such significance that it has impacted our business as a whole."
Private label (DSG, VRST, CALIA)/floor space:
- E1: "DICK'S is definitely expanding into private label products which are competing with our products. ... We're definitely monitoring very closely, as we would have to venture into different categories or into different parts of our business, if their private label would take market share from us."
- E3: "Now that they have private label, it is for sure impacting it in a negative way, but that impact has been absorbed over the last few years as their private label has been in place for a bit."
- E4: "I know when I was working with Reebok, they basically took all of our apparel out of store because they felt it was a direct competitor of their private label stuff."
- E5: "Whenever a retailer expands a private label, it's definitely competitive with branded business. It's never something that we love." / "you can never count out the VRST the Cleats from a DSG private label continuing to gain share."; "the things that can be done by a private label per se, we've seen those start to slow down."
- E7: "We have a strong graphic tee business. DICK'S has a strong graphic tee business, and floor space is just a general challenge. I would just say from one of our volume drivers, they're providing some direct competition with us."
- E10: "Not in our category necessarily, but in athletic, their private label is having a moment."
- E12: "If you asked me this question about a year ago, I would've said that it's competing. Right now, we've noticed that Dick's Sporting Goods, because of the loss of Nike, they had to target the everyday consumer... Right now, we have not seen any real diminishment or reduction in ordering or sales when it comes to product because we are not playing in the same space as Dick's Sporting Goods."
- E13: "I think they're very, very brand driven, so always, name brands are seeing better sell-through than private label."
- E11: "It's pretty neutral from a basketball brand perspective. They would not be able to create product that necessarily competes with the basketball brand product because this is a legacy brand. Consumers, they look for the Jumpman specifically."
- E8: "From what I've seen, it's more basics and activewear type product that is their private label."
- Compiler: no vendor reported incremental markdown money in Q2 except WC sock closeout ask; 2 product-specific margin asks; terms stable per 8 voices, tighter per 2 (E3 FL leverage; E14 seasonal supply-chain pass-through); private label = floor-space competitor per commodity, workwear, UA; neutral per premium/fashion footwear & basketball.

### 3e. Inventory / open-to-buy
- E1: "we have seen a more frequent order pattern from DICK'S versus previous years." / "they do reorders based on consumer demand."; "I would say for us, consistent, although from sources within the industry, I have heard that they have been buying quite aggressively."
- E2: "they are quite conservative in doing that in order to keep their cash constantly under control." / "They control very well their stock in store. They order accordingly, and they somehow forecasted a certain hype linked to the World Cup."; "maybe we will have a little bit more of stock in store, especially of the unsold team wear for the teams that were eliminated"
- E3: "Of the retailers that I work with, DICK'S Sporting Goods is extremely diligent with their inventory, and very good at managing it."
- E5: "We've definitely seen some less aggressive or a less aggressive posture from them on inventory than we have in the past. They've been more willing to chase or more likely to chase into product as opposed to buying it all upfront, and definitely some more asks around a partnership standpoint of they believe in something but want to have some back-end protection from definitely not receiving all of it all at once. A bit more focus around AR or auto-replenishment product, watching their terms and inventory levels more. We're seeing the demand hasn't necessarily changed as much, but they definitely seem to be more cautious that things could turn."; "if there's an item or style or collection that they like and are seeing momentum behind, they are still willing to take a big swing. In the past, sometimes they were a bit more broad in their swings, so they're definitely staying more focused."
- E6: "I would say that they're being fairly aggressive. They're winning in the marketplace. They've posted good comp results. Our denim trend is good. They've been fairly aggressive with trying to grow our business." / "Q1 was better than Q2. Q2, we lost a little bit of a leaky bucket. Now, we're into Q3, and we're trying to figure out if Q3 going to be as good as Q2, or is it going to leak anymore."
- E7: "there's just a lot of chasing back into those products."; "I think they want to move away from carryover business into more seasonal, but they want to be more reactive, and it's just harder to be more reactive."
- E9: "It looks like the way DICK'S is managing their business is just there is a big spike in August for back to school and the other months they have not anticipated what the demand will be for that yet." / "We do have very crystal clear clarity on orders, as I mentioned, for the August time period already in the system, and those are confirmed orders"
- E11: "What I mean by appropriate is that they tend to carry a larger on-hand inventory, but that's purposeful. Sometimes, they will over-invest in order to put together a large short wall, for example."; "If sales are slower, and that can be due to those macroeconomic factors, then you won't see as large of an open-to-buy."
- E12: "Having strong, aggressive inventory at this point in time is a positive thing to have." / "Another thing to factor in is Labor Day is coming up soon."; "I think the hardest thing to understand with DICK'S Sporting Goods is understanding their reorder phase and how they order back into product"; "We're probably going to see adjustments and changes in the next three months when it comes to maybe overselling in the summer period, or underselling and trying to get back into our best-selling products."; "I do not think any other retailer hits back to school in a athleisure or athlete space as DICK'S Sporting Goods does."
- E13: "No, their fall bookings remain intact, and they continue to ask us for more allocation and more doors." / "Consistent for the quarter, aggressive for the upcoming fall season."
- E14: "We are seeing a better forward commitment up front with DICK'S Sporting Goods. They have more confidence in their buys. We are also seeing their ability to come back in and replenish where they are seeing strong sales-through, as long as we have the inventory availability."; "We're primarily seeing that growth in that back-to-school consumer, the consumer that has a lot of confidence and the back-to-school, to back-to-sport, to back-to-college demand."
- E4: "We haven't seen their at-once business for the back half of the year, so we will be up substantially by year's end once we start seeing their at-once orders for the back half of Q3 and Q4."
- No cancellations reported (E13 "No change with the order book, with the quantity on any purchase order"; "their fall bookings remain intact").
- Compiler: OTB posture category-specific — aggressive buys/pull-forwards/chases in trend categories (UGG, workwear tees, denim, run); more cautious at largest branded supplier (UA: chase + back-end protection); commodity & WC = in-season replenishment + planned post-event slowdown; BTS orders confirmed & large; 2H visibility beyond Aug limited for at-once/reorders.

### 3f. Consumer & promotions
- E2: "There is a strong trend towards improvement in consumption for sporting goods, and DICK'S is doing particularly well."
- E3: "the war in the Middle East, during this quarter, hindered it at some level as gas prices skyrocketed. People were cutting back on what they had decided to spend" / "Those things are impacting basket size across all retailers, full stop."
- E4: "with the economy not in the best place, what's happened is they're very price sensitive and are looking for the cheapest of deals. We've seen a spike in our value channel because of that or they're going to our top-tier retail partners. ... It's cut out the middleman."
- E5: "We see and hear from the broader marketplace of softness in traffic, pressure on the consumer, but what we're seeing from a DICK'S standpoint is they continue to shop, continue to buy, and are continuing to buy more of the premium products. What we have seen that's probably a shift is that the bigger moments drive more volume than they have in the past, so you go through some more lulls and big bumps when you get to big selling time periods like a Father's Day."; "there definitely is a K-shape economy, a bifurcation of the consumer. ... That consumer is trading down from a performance or a price standpoint, where that DSG consumer is trading up and buying more pinnacle, more premium product, more innovation"; "DICK'S and the marketplace at large are definitely getting more promotional. There are more discounts to the consumer. That obviously affects margin to a certain extent. That's not the margin between us and them. That's more of a gross margin situation on their side."; "Consumers are definitely looking for newness. They're looking for a reason to pay premium prices. They're not just willing to do it on everything. They're definitely being more discerning than ever before."
- E6: "Elevated costs of doing business has impacted the demand as customers have had less discretionary income available to purchase apparel" / "They're getting higher average unit transactions for their products."; "The customer is looking for value because their discretionary income is under siege with paying more with, first, goods due to tariffs. They have less money due to gas and food prices. Wage growth has not seen that same growth."
- E7: "While you might see smaller cart sizes at DICK'S, they're filled with probably a few more items for the dollar. Instead of getting one T-shirt from a premium brand, you're getting two T-shirts from us or three or four from private label."
- E8: "Weather across the board has been really strong this spring and summer, so when the weather is good and warm, it drives traffic into stores generally"; "From the outside, just looking at their marketplace, it does not seem that they are overly promotional, but they seem healthy from the outside looking in."; Q1 strength "going to continue for the rest of the year" (per §4)
- E10: "No, traffic is up."; "We're seeing a shift from real bold, outlandish sorts of print and style to a more subtle, muted, and conservative consumer."
- E12: "We've actually seen an increase in basket size increases from what we've been hearing from the marketplace." / "The other big driver, which no one should overlook, is definitely July 4th weekend, this is where our biggest sale opportunities are."
- E13: "We are a MAP-protected brand, so we're not a promotional brand." / "The consumer is back to store, and they care about that."
- E14: "Their traffic is increasing. We are not seeing any decline in customer traffic, consumer traffic. We are not seeing any decline in purchase power or average price point per purchase." / "They're not observing any trading down from their consumer regarding price point tiers."
- No expert mentioned price matching. Compiler: traffic up/resilient per E5, E13, E14, E12, E10; macro pressure (gas, tariffs, food) per E12, E4, E6, E7 → smaller baskets/channel shifts; only E5 said DKS getting more promotional; E8 said not overly promotional.

### 3g. Competition
- E1: "We expect them, for us, to maintain their market share or their market position versus other retailers in the same segment."
- E2: "Definitely gain share." / "Foot Locker integration, product mix, and their disciplined approach to management of the stock."
- E3: "They are the strongest in the marketplace, but... It's a tough competition out there. I'm going to say that it's going to maintain the current market share"; "I think it would be really good in the U.S. if JD Sports continues to build out their presence"
- E4: "I could see them taking some market share from an Academy Sports. This is probably the closest direct competitor for both our boat shoes and sandals/clogs. ... I think they will take market share from Hibbett Sports. ... I think department stores like some of our partners like Kohl's, JCPenney, and Macy's could potentially lose some business to a DICK'S Sporting Goods."; "Some of the accounts that I think they could potentially lose share to would be like a SCHEELS. ... DSG could potentially lose some market share to marketplaces and Amazon. I say this due to their vendor direct expansion, expanded online assortments, marketplace style endless aisle capabilities" / "Maybe a bit to off-price retail, more so on the HEYDUDE side of the brand, if excess inventory enters the market for retailers like TJX, Ross, and Burlington" / "We're also doing a lot more business with Fanatics, which I think could impact the DICK'S business a little bit."; marketplaces "that could be walmart.com, plus.target.com, shop.tiktok.com."; "I don't want to use the term monopoly, but they're really establishing themselves as a presence within the sporting goods channel in particular. A lot of these other sporting goods accounts just aren't able to keep up and are falling by the wayside or losing market share."; "the public retailer data shows that DSG is gaining share"
- E5: "We are definitely hearing softness from some of their competitors... They are probably taking a little share from the Academys and stuff of the world as well."
- E6: "I think DICK'S Sporting Goods is a U.S. retailer who's really owned the full market space across all 50 states. There's no other really nationwide sporting goods retailer." / "Clubs have been a great segment of the business that's growing because they're getting traffic, off-pricers, premium, and elevated retailers. The biggest challenge has really been department stores and middle tier."
- E7: "I expect them to maintain share, but that is because companies like Academy and others are being very aggressive as well. There's a lot of opportunity with our brand, and I think all ships are rising."
- E11: "I wouldn't compare them to an Academy. The way that our segmentation strategy operates is that we don't create the same product for Academy Sports as we do for DICK'S Sporting Goods and Foot Locker."; "I know On has, obviously, been going into the court space. They are set to release a golf shoe. That's, again, going to continue to take up those things like mind share and open-to-buy, especially in a place like DICK'S Sporting Goods that also owns Golf Galaxy."
- E12: "their investment in their new store locations, their House of Sport, the Field House locations, like these are big pinnacle investments, and this is where I feel as though that Academy and other competitors just cannot beat." / "It is very hard to beat a dedicated run door that has people that run all the time, that are marathon runners"; "Usually, key performance or key pinnacle items belong in D2C because no one wants to take that risk. Dick's Sporting Goods have noticed that, no, they have to take the risk with brands right now"
- E13: "I think that DICK'S is broadening our consumer base, I don't think they're taking away our business from somebody else."
- E14: "Academy is one of their bigger competitors. We are seeing Academy really divest out of the key markets, the macro markets, and they are investing into some of the satellite market destinations where DICK'S won't invest." / "West Marine's Chapter 11 recently will open up real estate opportunities"
- E9: "That's a sale that's going to DICK'S that otherwise would have gone to maybe even like a Nordstrom."
- Compiler: 10 voices gain/maintain, none lose overall. Share donors: Academy, Hibbett, Kohl's/JCPenney/Macy's, regional sporting-goods chains, Nordstrom, West Marine. Share takers from DKS: SCHEELS, Amazon/marketplaces (walmart.com, target.com, TikTok Shop), off-price (TJX, Ross, Burlington, Nordstrom Rack), Fanatics, run specialty, brand DTC for pinnacle. Walmart/Target only as marketplaces and trade-down end of K-shape (E5).

### 3h. Relationship trajectory / mgmt quality
- E1: "their way of communication, their preparation, the way they communicate, the way they pay invoices, has been a very high standard to this date." / "over the past year, our relationship with DICK'S has only been strengthened."
- E2: "They're clear on their expectation. They try to give you perspective of what is happening with them, and they're quite reliable."
- E3: "They are a strong strategic partner, have been for over a decade plus... we're codependent." / "It can be challenging because they're very demanding." / "All of the above, assortment, pricing, you name it."
- E4: "They're one of our quicker partners to get products and attributes built out so that we can get product posted on their site and made purchasable." / "They don't often put product on their site too early ahead of a launch, which is a problem with some of our customers."
- E5: "From a forecasting accuracy standpoint, they are some of the best-in-class." / "We've had Ed Stack on campus for the first time in a while over the last few months. We've come off of some really strong selling meetings and had 40+ of their buyers and executives in our offices and making the trip down."; "They've definitely gotten more focused. With some of their leadership team updates, you can definitely see that they are picking more one brand to stand for something. ... the days of them having a left chest cotton hoodie from every brand have gone by the wayside."
- E6: "Really good partners. Good partners, good partnership, fans of the brand" / "I'd say it's stayed consistent at this point."
- E7: "I'd probably have to rate those separately. Forecast accuracy probably being low. We constantly have big changes from them on a regular basis. The other two, fairly high."
- E8: "In terms of communication, they are very good at communicating. It is all about people."
- E9: "Definitely strengthened. As we began to see success at retail, they were willing to put forward larger orders and take larger risks with us."
- E10: "Their buying teams are really easy to deal with. They communicate really well. They are real clear in their compliance"
- E11: "Pretty good partners at times... If for that particular quarter, if sales are down, the relationship is going to be a bit more strained. That's just the nature of working with retailers in this environment." / "I would say it's pretty consistent over the past year."
- E12: "their overall forecasting accuracy is great when it comes to initial load in. It's the reorders that get a little bit difficult." / "These communications have been long overdue, and I am glad that they are showcasing to the larger audience on what the future of DICK'S plus Foot Locker is going to be like"
- E13: "They have a lot of people with their hand in the pot from a buying perspective, but we have one person that manages the entire account, so that keeps it simple from our end" / "it becomes a relationship built on honesty and trust."; UGG forecast accuracy "pretty accurate" (per §5)
- E14: "As their strategies are redefined and refined, we're seeing that that has a definite impact on the executive team that we're in close contact with. Very favorable at this point."
- Compiler: 9 of 14 strengthened, 4 consistent, none weakened; forecast accuracy best-in-class (UA, UGG), "low" (workwear), great initial load-in/difficult reorders (E12); E5 & E12 tie improvement to leadership updates, Stack visit, post-acq clarity on FL & private label.

### 5. Contradictions (compiler)
- Between experts: (1) inventory posture — E5 "less aggressive... more cautious that things could turn" vs E7 "aggressive replan orders", E6 "fairly aggressive", E13 "managing inventory with us aggressively", E2 "quite conservative", E1 "consistent... heard... buying quite aggressively" → category-specific; (2) pricing/terms — E14 "more favorable"; E3 "tighter negotiation pressure due to the purchase of Foot Locker"; E5, E11, E10, E4 stable; E1 "tougher negotiations, especially on margins"; (3) promo — E5 more promotional vs E8 not overly vs E13 MAP "not a promotional brand"; (4) FL effect on orders — E5 no change/"pretty separate"; E14 "cleaned up their ordering process"; E3 "will purchase less"; E2 "might order more stock"; E1 "just a name on the order sheet"; (5) Nike — E12 renewed/Nike-forward; E5 mixed/slow lifestyle; E7 "Companies like Nike, North Face, Patagonia don't have the brand power that they used to"; E3 running on fire → performance strong vs lifestyle weak; (6) private label — commodity/workwear/UA competing; E12 stopped competing; E10 "having a moment"; E13 name brands sell through better; (7) forecast accuracy — UA best-in-class; UGG "pretty accurate"; workwear "low... constantly have big changes"; E12 great initial/difficult reorders; (8) consumer — UA & E14 trading up/no trade-down vs E3, E4, E6, E7 pressure/value-seeking/smaller baskets → bifurcated (UA "K-shape economy"); (9) share-loss channels — only E4 names Amazon/marketplaces, SCHEELS, off-price, Fanatics; E12 run specialty only.
- Experts vs mgmt: May mgmt "healthy consumer... no signs of trading down" (Hobart 2026-05-27) matched UA, E14, UGG; conflicted w/ E3, E4, E6, E7 macro views. May FL "first quarter of positive comps", "right on schedule" (Stack) echoed by E11, E14, E12; Aug print (FL −3.6%, loss) contradicted both, closest to E3. Aug "much more promotional"/"pricing investments"/"participating" anticipated only by UA; contradicted by E8 and E14. "Legacy footwear silhouettes" diagnosis consistent w/ E3, UA, E12, UGG, Crocs.
- Unverified: E12 "apparel sector... 25%-30% increase" and "half of their sales, to 30%... footwear, are all Nike" (no counterpart; 2nd internally inconsistent); E11 "they just increased their overall guidance" partially accurate (May raised comp low ends & DKS margin high end; non-GAAP EPS held, GAAP EPS cut).

### 6. Bias/reliability (compiler)
- AI-interviewer fixed script (role, categories, % rev, May-Jul sell-in, Aug-Oct forecast, CY forecast, share, pricing, cost inputs, private label, categories, assortment shifts, allocation, innovation, Fast Break, partnership, relationship trajectory); interviewer paraphrases in leading/affirming language ("That's a strong endorsement", "That's a clear and compelling case", "Strong local execution combined with overall business momentum"), expert agrees "Yes."; some asked repeats or answered wrong period (Crocs answered Aug-Oct w/ Q4; denim mixed Q1/Q2 & Aug-Oct) → period attributions caveated.
- Supplier bias: all 14 suppliers; AlphaSense labels all "Partner"; 9 strengthened, none weakened. 4 new/near-new accounts (uniform socks since early 2026, premium apparel 2026, fashion boots since 2H 2025, UGG "a couple of years") → triple-digit growth = base effects. UGG, Crocs control own sell-in ("their business model remains scarcity in the marketplace. They control their growth") → measure brand allocation not DKS OTB.
- E3 role label discrepancy (title says retail shoe chain; content supplier-side) → no competing-retailer voice in set; file renamed `DKS_Expert-Call_2026-07-06_Executive_Footwear-Brand-Planner_forecasting-logistics.md`.
- E10 split from E9 export (no headline/metadata) → `DKS_Expert-Call_2026-07-14_SECOND-INTERVIEW-same-export_Casual-Golf-Swim-Apparel-Director.md`.
- All calls predate Q2 period end (2026-08-01); latest (07-28, 07-29) in final week of qtr.
- Most direct exposure: (i) UA (E5) "hundreds of millions of dollars", futures-based receipts, Stack visit, 40+ buyers; (ii) E14 "About 18%" highest concentration; (iii) E3 5-15%, account demand planning/inventory/logistics; (iv) E12, E11 direct FL+DKS exposure but "single digits"/"less than a couple percentage points"; (v) E13 doesn't manage DKS account directly ("I manage the other half of the business, but I work very closely with the manager on the DICK'S account").
- Second-hand claims: E1 "from sources within the industry"; E12 "from an outsider looking in" + 25-30% apparel + 30%/half Nike; E8 relies on "their earnings calls or their public information" + store walks; E9 "We don't sell to a ton of retailers that would be in the same specific of marketplace as DICK'S"; E11 "they just increased their overall guidance"; E4 "the public retailer data shows that DSG is gaining share".
- Sample gaps: no Nike-corporate, adidas, On, HOKA, Deckers-corporate, hard-goods (golf, team equipment, fitness, trading cards) suppliers; no FL EMEA exposure; no landlord, logistics, ex-DKS employee views. Apparel over-represented (8/14); footwear dominated by casual/brown-shoe (Crocs/HEYDUDE, UGG x2, fashion boots) + 1 basketball + 1 Nike-type.
- Transcription artefacts: "450" for "Foot Locker"; "Adi" for adidas; "Stealthform"/"StealthForm".

### 7. Top-15 pitch quotes =same as above (UA promo; UA inventory posture; E3 "purchase less"; UA Nike mixed; E3 sportswear tough; E12 FL inventory hit; E12 Nike-forward; E14 edit to amplify/SKU 20-30%; E11 HoS non-comp; E13 238%/150%; E7 front-load; E3 negotiation power; UA K-shape; E4 "monopoly"; UA Stack visit/40+ buyers). Reserve: E7 graphic tee floor space; E2 WC closeout; E11 smaller OTB if sales slower; E12 dedicated run door; E10 specific inbound margin.
